How	This	Document	Is	Organized This edition consolidates the controlled build package into eight Books. Content is unchanged from the released versions except for this front matter and the Workflow Automation section; where a Book’s text says “this volume” or “companion volume”, read it as the corresponding Book of this document. Cross-references of the form MFG/MM followed by a Part or section number resolve inside Books I and II respectively; INV and ACC remain external released specifications. Book Content Role Front Matter Unified revision history, module index, Workflow Automation specification (Section W) Orientation and shared services Book I — Manufacturing Core Parts A–K: inventory gap closure (RMA, Sales Tab, Packing, Inspection Plans, Count Documents, Cost Variance Analysis, Fiscal Periods, Safety Stock, Tax), Order Management, Engineering & BOM & CAD, Production Management & MRP, Quality Management, Barcoding & Traceability, Procurement, Cost & Profit Centers, Costing & Pricing, GL integration map, data model & roadmap 
Field level functional authority 
Book II — Material Management Parts A–H: material model & Material Master views, creation & change governance, valuation & automatic account determination, cross-module linkage contracts, special stock & ownership, batch & serial profiles, mass maintenance & migration & data quality, MM data model 
Field level functional authority 
Book III — Enterprise Completeness Parts A–H: cross-volume flow closure book (eight end-to-end flows), integration & transaction architecture, security & IAM, resilience & operations, finite scheduling & capable-to-promise, multi-plant & intercompany & global model, CAD/PDM/PLM integration, test & migration & cutover & acceptance 
Enterprise architecture authority 
Book IV — Design & Development Completion Controlling baseline and precedence, scope decision register DEC-01…10, conflict register, consolidated flows FL-09/10/11, executable pricing condition model, production versions and planning algorithm baseline, module completion additions, architecture decision records, design and development gates, authoring templates, action register 
Governance and delivery 
Book V — Designer’s Guide & Visual Atlas SAP S/4HANA capability map and plain-language glossary, structure and flow diagrams, master data atlas, module design briefs with screen inventories, barcoding hardware and label specification, CAD linkage visuals, design system starter Governance and delivery 
Book VI — Screen by Screen Implementation Plan Seventy eight implementation entries across fifteen module sections with routes, layouts, actions, arrivals, outputs, postings, scans, build waves and field authorities. Seven wave build sequence. Consolidated Costing and Pricing section. Linkage spine table 
Governance and delivery 
Book VII — Go-Live Readiness Supplement Gap register closure crosswalk, supplemental screen entries, multi plant planning setup screens and network behaviors, controlled build package manifest, data migration package governance, complete barcode sample book with usage matrix, go live readiness statement and execution directive 
Governance and delivery 
Book VIII — Design Execution Handbook Designer operating manual: the eight screen archetypes with wireframes, the lead to delivery business flow sheet, BPMN swimlanes and sequence diagrams for the five governing flows, consolidated inventory, warehousing and accounting detail, 
Designer and program operating manual

Book IX Module grade specifications with activation evidence per capability 
W.	Workflow	Automation Every governed action named in this document — approvals, task routing, escalations, alerts and scheduled jobs — executes on one shared automation layer. ACC Section 22 built the approval engine for finance documents; this section generalizes it as the platform service all Books use, and catalogs every workflow so none exists as bespoke code. No module may implement its own approval or notification mechanism. W.1	Approval	Workflow	Engine Element Specification Definition model Per document type: trigger (submit, threshold breach, field-change class), condition rules (value bands, return class, margin floor delta, warranty flag, entity — any document field via the rule grammar), approver chain (sequential steps; each step = role, named user, or derived owner such as Cost Center Manager), parallel steps where flagged (CCB votes), and terminal actions (status transition, posting release, document generation) 

=== PAGE 11 ===
ENTERPRISE ERP — UNIFIED BUILD SPECIFICATION  |  V1.0 CONFIDENTIAL 
CONFIDENTIAL Page 11 of 312 
Element Specification Versioning Workflow definitions are effective-dated and versioned; in-flight instances complete on the version they started; changes require the owning role in W.4 plus Controller when the workflow gates a posting Delegation and absence Per-user delegate with date range; delegation logged on every action taken; standing delegation of financial approvals above $250k prohibited (SoD rule) Escalation and SLA Per step: SLA hours, reminder cadence, escalation target (role manager) on breach; breaches feed the exception dashboards (e.g. MCR SLA per Book II §4.2, RMA aging per Book I Part A §3.3) Recall and resubmit Initiator may recall before first approval; rejection returns with mandatory comments to the responsible step; resubmission preserves history Acting surface In-app queue (ACC 22 pattern), email actions with signed links, and mobile-responsive approval view; every action records user, timestamp, channel and comment on the immutable audit trail Evidence Approval history renders on the document Activity tab and exports with it; Part H acceptance evidence (Book III) cites these records W.2	Notification	and	Alert	Engine Generalizes the alert rules screen (INV 15.11): rule = event or scheduled condition + scope filter + severity + recipients (roles/users) + channels (in-app, email; SMS reserved) + dedupe window. All alerts named across the Books register here: below safety stock, calibration due, unconfirmed POs, placeholder aging, promise-at-risk, DLQ depth, SLO burn, certificate expiry, count SLA, close checklist blockers. Alert fatigue control: per-rule dedupe, digest option for non-critical severities, and a monthly noise review owned by each rule’s owner. W.3	Scheduled	Job	Framework All background processing runs on the batch framework specified in Book III Part D §5 (run history, SLA timers, dependency chains, retries, escalation). The standing job catalog: nightly MRP; ADU and ABC/XYZ classification (monthly); batch expiry sweep (daily); vendor rating rollup (daily); economic nexus monitor (daily); allocation cycles, warranty accrual, E&O run (period-end); costing runs (per policy); recalc queue for tax failover (15 min); data-quality KPI computation (daily); access recertification generation (quarterly). Jobs declare their close-criticality so the period-close gate (FL-06) can hold on failures automatically. W.4	Workflow	Catalog	(binding	register) Workflow Trigger / Chain Summary Specified In Owner PO approval ladder Value bands: Buyer ≤ $25k → Purchasing Mgr ≤ $250k → Controller → CFO > $1M Book I Part G §1 Purchasing Mgr 
PR approval (manual/expense) Cost-center + budget check routing Book I Part G §3; ACC 21 Controller Credit release Credit hold → Finance queue (release / reject / raise limit) Book I Part B §2.2 Controller Price floor breach Below floor: Pricing Mgr → Sales Director → CFO (floor −5pts) Book I Part I §6.3 Pricing Mgr Quotation internal review Margin below target → estimator + pricing approval Book I Part B §3.3 Sales Director

1. Purpose, Scope and Document Set This volume is the definitive, field-by-field implementation specification for the PE Transformer ERP manufacturing core. It is written to the same standard, structure and depth as the two companion volumes already in the development partner’s hands, and the volumes of the PE Transformer ERP Build Package together form the controlled build baseline (Phase 1 functional scope with an architected target-state path; see Enterprise Completeness Spec V1.0): Volume Covers Status Inventory & Warehouse Module Spec V3.0 Material Master, warehousing, transactions, serial/batch tracking, counting, valuation screens, inventory GL map (Sections 1–15) Released — build in progress Accounting Module Implementation Spec V2.0 AR, AP, GL, bank reconciliation, tax engine, intercompany, budgeting, workflows, RBAC, ASC 606, batch payments, period close (Sections 2–26) Released 
This Book — Manufacturing Core Order Management, Engineering & BOM, Production Management, Quality Management, Barcoding & Traceability, Procurement, RMA, Cost/Profit Centers, Costing & Pricing, and closure of every item in the Post-Inventory Design Review gap register 
This document 
 Every screen in this volume is specified with: exact fields with data types, requirement level and validation rules; every button and its enabled/disabled conditions; status lifecycles with allowed transitions and colors; GL posting rules with the account numbers already established in the CoA (Inventory Spec Screen 11.4 and Accounting Spec Section 13); cost center and profit center derivation for every financially relevant posting; barcode scan points; role access; error and exception handling; and integration callouts to the other two volumes. Where a screen from a companion volume is referenced, the reference is given as “INV” (Inventory Spec V3) or “ACC” (Accounting Spec V2) plus the section number. Dev Note: Nothing in this volume changes INV or ACC. Engineering creates and revises items and BOMs; Purchasing sources them; Production consumes and produces them; Sales prices and sells them; Inventory stores and tracks them; Costing values them; Accounting posts them. The flow below governs which module owns which attribute of an item. Ownership means only that module’s screens may edit the attribute; all others render it read-only with a link to the owning screen. Attribute Group (Material Master tab) Owning Module / Role Consumed By General Information, Dimensions, Tracking config Inventory Admin (INV 2.2/2.3) All modules BOM tab (read-only view) Engineering (Part C) — edits happen in EBOM/MBOM screens Production, Costing, Purchasing (for shortage checks) Purchasing tab, AVL, lead times Purchasing (Part G) MRP, Reorder planning, Costing Sales tab, price lists, customer prices Sales Admin (Part A §4) Quotations, Sales Orders, Invoices

3. RMA — Return from Customer PE Transformer’s customer return process is the RMA process. No customer material enters any PE warehouse without an approved RMA number. The RMA module replaces the bare “Return from Customer” transaction stub: the inventory movement specified in the gap register (DR Return Inventory / CR COGS reversal) becomes one step inside a controlled, end-to-end process that also handles warranty determination, inspection, disposition, credit memos, repairs, replacements and field returns of installed transformers. 3.1 Return Classes Class Typical Case Key Differences RC1 — Component / distribution return PE Grid Components customer returns bushings, arresters, hardware bought from stock Standard receipt into quarantine; restocking fee rules apply; credit or replacement; fastest path RC2 — Finished transformer, not yet energized Unit refused at delivery, damaged in transit, wrong configuration shipped Serialized; freight and crating logistics; transit-damage insurance claim fields; full inspection RC3 — Field return of installed / energized unit Warranty failure at substation; unit de-energized and returned for teardown or repair Warranty adjudication from serial record; oil handling and hazmat transport; teardown inspection; repair order or replacement unit; failure analysis feeding NCR/CAPA RC4 — Return for billable repair / refurbishment Out-of-warranty unit sent in for repair at customer cost No credit; generates a billable Repair Order and a service invoice (4150 Service & Repair Revenue)  3.2 End-to-End Process Flow 1. Request. Customer service creates the RMA from the originating document (invoice, sales order or shipped serial). System pulls customer, item, serial/batch, ship date, warranty status. If no reference document exists, the No-Reference exception path (§3.9) applies. 2. Approval. Approval routing per RMA policy matrix (§3.10): value threshold, return class and warranty claim flag decide the approver chain (Customer Service Manager, then Quality Manager for RC3, then Controller above $50,000 expected credit). 3. Authorization issued. RMA number and return instructions (PDF) are emailed to the customer: RMA barcode label to affix, ship-to dock, freight terms (who pays per policy), packaging/oil handling instructions for RC3. 4. Return receipt. Receiving scans the RMA barcode; the receipt screen (a Check-In of Receipt Type “Return Receipt”, DELTA to INV 5.1) is pre-populated from RMA lines. All returned stock lands in the Quarantine warehouse with quality status QI Hold, GL account 1335 Return/RMA Inventory. Serial status moves to Returned. 5. Inspection. A Return Inspection (Quality module, reusing the Incoming Inspection engine INV 6.1 with plan type = RMA) is auto-created per line. For RC3, the teardown checklist applies and photographs are mandatory attachments. 6. Disposition. Inspector proposes, authorized role approves: Restock / Repair / Replace / Scrap / Return to Customer As-Is. Warranty claims additionally get Warranty Approved or Warranty Denied. 7. Settlement. Depending on disposition: credit memo request → ACC credit note (ACC Section 4); replacement sales order at zero price with reference; repair order (rework production order, Part D §9) either under 2610 Warranty Reserve or billable; scrap posting; or return shipment to customer. 8. Closure. RMA closes when every line reaches a terminal disposition and all financial documents are posted. Closure is blocked while any linked document (credit note, WO, replacement SO) is open.

3.5 Screen RMA-003 — Return Receipt (DELTA to INV 5.1) Receiving opens the receipt directly by scanning the RMA barcode on the arriving crate (scan point SP-19, Part F). The Check-In engine is reused with Receipt Type = Return Receipt and these deltas: Delta Specification Header reference RMA # is mandatory; vendor fields hidden; customer shown instead Line source Lines pre-populated from RMA authorized lines; receiving may receive partial quantities (creates back-order on RMA line, status Partially Received) Over-receipt Received Qty > Authorized Qty is blocked (hard error E-RMA-03). Unexpected extra material is booked on a new line flagged Unauthorized Return, which forces a supervisor override and its own inspection Condition on arrival Per line: dropdown (Undamaged / Damaged Packaging / Damaged Product / Leaking Oil) + mandatory photo attachment when not Undamaged Destination Location restricted to the Quarantine warehouse from the RMA header; putaway rules engine (INV 15.6) proposes bin inside quarantine zone Quality status Always QI Hold; a Return Inspection is auto-created per received line Serial handling Scanned serial must equal authorized serial (E-RMA-04 on mismatch); serial lifecycle status set to Returned; location updated  Accounting Entry — RMA Receipt (stock item, credit expected) Debit Credit Amount Basis Notes 1335 Return / RMA Inventory 5120 COGS (reversal) Received Qty × valuation rate at original issue Implements and supersedes the gap-register journal; “Return Inventory” = 1335. Cost center: none; Profit center: from returned item’s product line  Accounting Entry — RMA Receipt (warranty repair expected, unit remains customer property) Debit Credit Amount Basis Notes No entry No entry — Customer-owned unit held for repair is tracked as non-valuated customer stock (quantity only, value $0). Only RC3/RC4 lines flagged Customer Property take this path  Dev Note: Distinguish ownership at receipt: if the commercial outcome will be credit or replacement, PE takes ownership back and the value entry above posts. If the unit is in for repair and returns to the same customer, ownership never transfers; the system books a non-valuated receipt (quantity tracking, zero value) so no COGS reversal ever posts. The Expected Disposition field defaults this flag: Credit/Replace/Restock → PE ownership; Repair → customer property. The flag is editable at receipt by Receiving Supervisor only. 3.6 Screen RMA-004 — Inspection & Disposition The inspection reuses the Incoming Inspection execution screen (INV 6.1.1) with plan type RMA (Part A §6). Credit basis falls back to current price list less 20% (config) and Controller approval is mandatory for any credit. This sequence is implemented once as the Pricing Service and called by all documents; it must never be re-implemented per document. 1. Manual override on the document line, if entered and within the user’s override authority (below floor → approval workflow), always wins and is stamped Price Source = Manual. 2. Active customer-specific price for the document’s customer, valid on the pricing date (order date by default; configurable to requested ship date). Price Source = Customer Agreement. 3. Active rate from the price list assigned to the customer (Customer Master → Price List field), applying quantity breaks. Price Source = Price List. 4. Item pricing method fallback (INV 2.3.3 Sub-section C): Fixed Price, or Markup over current cost. Price Source = Item Default. 5. No price found → line blocks with E-SAL-01 “No valid price for item {code} / customer {name} on {date}” — the document cannot be submitted with unpriced lines. Rule Specification Inheritance & override display The SO line shows the resolved price plus a Price Source badge; hovering shows the full waterfall (which levels existed and which one won). Overrides display both original and override values Repricing Quotation → SO copy carries quoted prices frozen (Price Source = Quotation); explicit Reprice button re-runs determination with confirmation diff dialog. Invoice always inherits SO price — never reprices silently Currency Rates convert only at document level using the daily rate table (ACC Sec 3); price masters never auto-convert

5. Packing Packing converts picked stock into shippable Handling Units (HUs) with weights, dimensions, labels and a packing list. For PE this ranges from a carton of gaskets to a 40-ton transformer on a dedicated flatbed with separate oil totes and accessory crates. Packing sits between Picklist (INV 5.7) and Shipment (Part B §5); goods remain in Finished Goods stock (no GL impact) until post-goods-issue at shipment. 5.1 Handling Unit Model Concept Specification Handling Unit (HU) A numbered physical package: HU-{SEQ:8} with Code128/SSCC barcode. Types: Carton, Crate, Skid/Pallet, Reel, Tote, Flatbed Load, Container. An HU contains item quantities and/or other HUs (one level of nesting: cartons on a pallet) Contents Lines of item + qty + batch/serial. Serialized FG: exactly one transformer serial per primary HU Attributes Tare weight, net weight, gross weight (validated: gross ≈ tare + net ± 2%), dimensions L×W×H, packaging material item (consumes 1360/packaging stock via backflush), seal numbers, hazmat flags (oil = UN3082 where applicable), nitrogen pressure (transformers shipped gas-filled), impact recorder serial Statuses Open (packing) → Closed (contents locked, label printed) → Loaded (assigned to shipment) → Shipped. Reopen allowed until Loaded, logs reason  5.2 Screen PCK-001 — Packing Queue Screen ID OM-PACK-LIST-001 URL Pattern /sales/packing Purpose Everything picked and awaiting packing, and all in-progress pack orders  Column Specification Pack Order # PK-{YYYY}-{SEQ:5}; auto-created when a picklist completes (config: per picklist / per shipment) Source Picklist #, Sales Order #, Shipment # links Customer / Ship-To Name and destination city Lines / Qty Outstanding lines and quantities to pack Required Ship Date From SO; red when today or past Status Not Started (gray), In Progress (yellow), Packed (green), Cancelled (red) Packer Assigned user HUs Count of HUs created  5.3 Screen PCK-002 — Pack Station Screen ID OM-PACK-STATION-001 URL Pattern /sales/packing/{packOrderId} Purpose Scan-driven packing execution at the pack bench or shipping bay

=== PAGE 37 ===
ENTERPRISE ERP — UNIFIED BUILD SPECIFICATION  |  V1.0 CONFIDENTIAL 
CONFIDENTIAL Page 37 of 312 
Role Rights Finance Approver (Finance Manager) Second approval for variances beyond value tolerance (§7.5); views valuation impact Inventory Admin Everything incl. void; cannot count documents they generated when SOD control is ON  7.4 Reconciliation Logic 1. Final Qty per line = Recount Qty when a recount exists, else Count Qty. If recount differs from first count by more than the recount threshold again, a third count is forced with supervisor + finance jointly present (rule R-CNT-03); third count is final. 2. Variance = Final Qty − Expected Qty; Variance Value = Variance × current valuation rate; both computed at reconciliation, not entry, so late valuation changes do not skew entered documents. 3. Movements posted between snapshot and entry (Soft Freeze mode) are replayed: Expected Qty is adjusted by the net of interim movements before variance is computed; the adjustment is shown as a separate “Interim Movements” column for audit. 4. Batch/serial reconciliation: for batch lines the count is per batch; a batch found that is not on the sheet is added as an Unlisted Batch line (requires supervisor confirmation and creates batch record correction). Serials are scanned; missing serials list and found-but-unexpected serials list are generated automatically. 5. Zero-count confirmation: a count of 0 against non-zero expected requires an explicit “Confirm Empty Location” action, not just a blank. 7.5 Tolerance and Approval Rules Rule Default Behavior Recount threshold ±2% or ±$500 per line Beyond either → recount mandatory Supervisor approval band Variance value ≤ $2,000 per line and ≤ $10,000 per document Supervisor approves alone Finance approval band Above supervisor band, or any ABC class-A item variance Sequential approval: Supervisor → Finance Manager Controller band Document net variance > $25,000 Third approval by Controller ABC-driven tolerances A: 0.5% / B: 2% / C: 5% qty tolerance Within tolerance lines auto-approve at reconciliation (flagged Auto-Approved)  7.6 Posting Rules On Post, the system creates one Stock Adjustment (INV 5.8) per count document, adjustment source = Physical Count / Cycle Count, lines for every non-zero variance, and posts it immediately (approvals already satisfied at the count layer — no second approval loop). 8. Cost Variance Analysis Screen ID INV-VALUATION-CVA-001 URL Pattern /inventory/valuation/cost-variance Purpose Single workbench for every cost variance the system generates: purchase price, invoice price, material usage, labor rate, labor efficiency, overhead absorption, subcontract. Referenced by the Pricing & Costing tab (INV 2.3.3 E); this section defines the formulas, drill-downs, layouts, postings and edge cases the gap register found undefined  8.1 Variance Types and Formulas Code Variance Formula Posted When GL (DR if unfavorable) PPV Purchase Price Variance (PO unit price − standard cost) × received qty GRN completion (standard-cost items) 5210 vs 2110 GR/IR path per INV Sec 12 #3 IPV Invoice Price Variance (Invoice price − PO price) × invoiced qty Three-way match posting (ACC 6) 5210 (sub-tagged IPV) vs 2110 MUV Material Usage Variance (Actual component qty issued − standard qty per released MBOM × good output) × component standard cost Production order settlement (Part D §8) 5220 vs 1320 WIP 
LRV Labor Rate Variance (Actual labor rate − work center standard rate) × actual hours WO settlement 5230 vs 1320 LEV Labor Efficiency Variance (Actual hours − standard hours for good output) × standard rate WO settlement 5240 vs 1320 OAV Overhead Absorption Variance Applied overhead (std rate × std driver for output) − actual overhead charged to order WO settlement 5250 vs 1320 
SPV Subcontract Price Variance (Subcontract invoice − standard subcontract cost) × qty Subcontract GRN/invoice 5260 vs 2110 RVV Revaluation On-hand qty × (new std − old std) Standard cost release (INV 8.2) 5330 vs 1310/1330  Sign convention everywhere: positive = unfavorable (actual exceeded standard). Favorable variances post as credits to the same accounts. All variance postings carry cost center (from work center for LRV/LEV/OAV, from warehouse for PPV/RVV, from production cost center for MUV) and profit center (product line of the parent item). 8.2 Workbench Layout Zone Content Filter bar Period (month, range), Entity, Variance types (multi), Item / Item Group, Vendor (PPV/IPV/SPV), Work Center (labor/overhead), Production Order, Cost Center, Profit Center, only-above-threshold toggle Summary tiles One tile per variance type: period total with favorable/unfavorable color, % of standard cost base, trend sparkline (6 periods)

Variance always posts to the same period as its source transaction — never split Zero/negative quantities Return-to-vendor and reversals generate mirror-image variance postings referencing the original variance line (flag Reversal) Currency Variance computed in entity currency using the source document’s booked rate; no retrospective FX restatement (FX effects live in ACC 7100, never in 52xx) Partial deliveries / partial invoices PPV recognized per receipt; IPV per invoice match on matched quantity only Standard change mid-period Receipts before release use old standard, after use new; RVV posts once at release for on-hand stock (INV 8.2 rule) — the workbench shows both segments of the period split by effective date

9. Fiscal Period Control (Inventory & Operations) Screen ID INV-CONFIG-PERIOD-001 URL Pattern /inventory/configuration/fiscal-periods Purpose Operational period gate for all stock-relevant modules, aligned one-to-one with the finance fiscal periods (ACC 26.1). Defines exactly which transactions each period status blocks, the open/close sequence, back-posting policy, permissions and error messages  9.1 Model ▸ Periods are owned by Finance (ACC 26.1: Open / Soft Close / Hard Close / Year-End Closed). The inventory statuses Open / Closing / Closed shown on INV 8.3 map to Open / Soft Close / Hard Close respectively — one underlying period record per entity per month, two presentations. No independent inventory calendar exists. ▸ Every posting transaction in every module validates the period of its posting date at save and again at post (race-safe: validation inside the posting transaction). ▸ Subledger-before-GL sequence: the inventory period must reach Closed (Hard) before the finance period can Hard Close — enforced by ACC 26.2 checklist step 6 (subledger reconciliation) plus a new step 6b “Inventory period closed”. 9.2 Module Behavior Matrix Module / Transaction Open Closing (Soft) Closed (Hard) GRN / Check-In, RMA receipt Post Post with warning banner Blocked Check-Out / issues, transfers, scrap Post Post with warning Blocked Stock adjustments, count postings Post Post with warning; count postings for the closing period allowed (that is their purpose) Blocked 
Production confirmations, WO settlement Post Settlement allowed (period-end activity); new confirmations warned Blocked Standard cost release / revaluation Post Blocked (must land in an open period) Blocked Sales PGI / shipment Post Post with warning Blocked Invoices / credit notes (ACC) Post Per ACC soft-close rule Blocked per ACC Master data changes Always allowed — masters are not period-controlled — — 
Reservations, picklists, packing (no GL) Always allowed — logistics documents without value posting are not period-blocked; only their posting steps are 
— —

CONFIDENTIAL Page 42 of 312 
9.3 Open/Close Sequence 1. Day −2: Finance sets period to Soft Close. All operational posting continues with warnings; teams clear WIP documents (Draft GRNs, open counts, unsettled WOs) driven by the Pre-Close Validation list (INV 8.3 step 1). 2. Day +1 to +3: cutoff cleanup — receipts dated correctly (goods physically received before month-end but keyed after are posted with posting date = actual receipt date, allowed during Soft Close with warning). 3. Inventory close run (INV 8.3): pre-close validation must return zero blockers (list with document links; each row has an owner and a Resolve button), valuation snapshot generated, closing JE posted, status → Closed. 4. Finance completes ACC 26.2 checklist; period Hard Closes. Next period was already opened automatically at first Soft Close (always exactly one Open and at most one Soft Close period per entity — rule R-PER-01). 5. Reopen: Closed → Soft Close requires Controller + CFO (ACC roles); the reopen reason and every posting made during a reopen window are tagged Reopened Posting on the audit trail and listed in the close report. 9.4 Back-Posting Policy Scenario Policy Posting date in current Soft Close period Allowed for all operational roles, warning banner “Period {p} is closing — posting requires month-end review”, logged Posting date in prior Closed period Blocked for everyone. No exceptions at the transaction layer. Corrections happen as current-period postings with Reference Date = original date (field available on adjustments, GRN and JE) so operational reports can restate while GL integrity holds Posting date in future period Blocked beyond next Open period (E-PER-04) to prevent fat-finger year typos  9.5 Permissions and Standard Errors Item Specification Set Soft Close / reopen to Open Controller Set Hard Close Controller after checklist green; system enforces prerequisite completion Reopen Hard Closed Controller + CFO dual action (two distinct users, sequential confirm) E-PER-01 “Posting date {date} falls in period {period}, status Closed. Post to the current open period and set Reference Date if needed.” E-PER-02 “Period {period} is in Soft Close. Your posting will be included in month-end review.” (warning, not blocking) E-PER-03 “Inventory period cannot Hard Close: {n} unresolved documents. Open the Pre-Close Validation list.” E-PER-04 “Posting date {date} is beyond the next open period.”

10. Safety Stock Screen ID INV-PLAN-SAFETY-001 URL Pattern /inventory/planning/safety-stock Purpose Definition, maintenance and planning behavior of buffer stock, per item per warehouse, including overrides, alerts and the exact arithmetic used by replenishment and MRP  10.1 Where Safety Stock Lives and Who May Edit It Safety Stock (Qty + UoM) exists per item per warehouse (the single item-master field from INV 2.2 becomes the default seeded into each warehouse row). The maintenance screen is a grid — one row per item/warehouse — editable inline by Production Planner and Inventory Admin; changes above 25% delta route to Planning Manager approval. Every change is audited with reason code (Demand Change, Lead Time Change, Service Level Change, New Item, Annual Review). 10.2 Planning Arithmetic Rule Specification Reorder point ROP = (Average Daily Usage × Replenishment Lead Time Days) + Safety Stock. ADU from trailing 90-day consumption (INV 9.1 field); lead time = vendor lead time for buy items, production lead time for make items Replenishment trigger Available (On-hand − Reserved − QI Hold − Blocked) + On-Order ≤ ROP → reorder proposal for (Max Stock Level − Available − On-Order), minimum = Reorder Qty, rounded to order multiple MRP treatment Safety stock is a floor on projected available balance: net requirement in bucket t = max(0, gross requirement + safety stock − projected available). MRP never plans to consume below safety stock; a projected dip below SS raises exception message SS-BREACH on the MRP Workbench (Part D §3) instead Static vs statistical Phase 1: static values maintained here. Phase 2 (flagged, not in current build): suggested SS = Z(service level) × σ(daily demand) × √(lead time); screen already reserves read-only columns Suggested SS and Service Level for that engine Expiry-managed items For FEFO shelf-life items, SS proposals cap at (shelf life ÷ 2) × ADU to avoid buffering into expiry write-offs; warning W-SS-02 when a manual value exceeds the cap  10.3 Overrides Override Specification Per-warehouse The grid row itself; blank row inherits the item default and shows it grayed with “(default)” Per-customer reservation buffer Optional rows: Customer + Item + Warehouse + Reserved Buffer Qty + Valid From/To. Implemented as a standing Material Reservation (INV 5.5, source type = Customer Buffer) auto-maintained by the system: the reservation renews as stock is consumed against it. Used for utility frame agreements guaranteeing spare availability. Reservation reduces Available like any other Seasonal profiles Optional 12 monthly multipliers per row (default 1.0); effective SS in month m = base SS × multiplier[m]

10.4 Alerts, Exceptions and Reporting ▸ Alert “Below Safety Stock” (extends INV 15.11 triggers): Available < effective SS → Critical severity, notify Planner + Purchasing real-time; distinct from Below Reorder Point which remains a Warning. ▸ Stock Projection Report (INV 15.10) draws the safety stock line using the effective (seasonal) value; projected crossings listed under the chart with dates. ▸ Exception report SS-EX-01: items whose SS > Max Stock Level, SS with zero ADU (dead buffer), SS never breached in 12 months (over-buffered candidates), SS breached ≥ 3 times in 90 days (under-buffered). ▸ Dashboard tile on Inventory Dashboard: count of active SS breaches with drill-down.

11. Tax Configuration Screen ID INV-CONFIG-TAX-001 URL Pattern /inventory/configuration/tax (sidebar Configuration → Tax Configuration) Purpose The operational tax determination layer that connects Material Master tax data, customer/vendor tax profiles and the Avalara engine (ACC Section 19). ACC 19 owns tax codes, GL mapping and filing; this screen owns determination policy and region handling for documents created in logistics modules  11.1 Determination Precedence Every taxable document line (quotation, SO, invoice request, vendor bill from PO) resolves tax input parameters in this order — each step only fills parameters the previous steps left empty: 1. Document-line manual tax override (role-limited: Finance only; reason mandatory; flagged on the document and in the Avalara Transaction Log as Override). 2. Customer exemption certificate (ACC 19.2): a Verified, unexpired certificate for the ship-to state sets exemption code → Avalara returns $0 for exempt lines. 3. Customer/vendor tax profile (§11.3): default tax code, nexus-relevant registration numbers, use-tax accrual flag for vendors. 4. Item tax data: Tax Category (Taxable / Tax-Exempt / Zero-Rated) and Avalara product tax code from the Sales tab (§4.1); HSN/HTS code for customs classification. 5. Jurisdiction: ship-from (warehouse address) + ship-to (document address) sent to Avalara for rate lookup; PE nexus configuration (§11.4) decides whether tax is collected at all. 6. Failover: Avalara unreachable → tax code override rate (ACC 19.1.1 Rate Override) with Override badge; documents post and are queued for recalculation (background retry every 15 min; discrepancies on recalc create tax adjustment JEs and appear in the Tax Reconciliation report ACC 19.3). 11.2 Configuration Screen Fields Field / Panel Specification Tax Engine Mode Avalara (default) / Manual Tables. Manual mode exposes a rate table (Jurisdiction, Rate %, Valid From/To) for pre-integration operation and test systems Default Tax Codes Per document family: Sales (S1), Purchases (U1 use tax), Intercompany (E-IC exempt), RMA credits (inherit original invoice’s codes — hard rule, not configurable) Calculation Trigger Points Quote save (estimate), SO save (estimate), Invoice post (commit), Credit note post (ReturnInvoice), Void (DocVoided) — mirrors ACC 19.1.2; checkboxes allow disabling estimates (not commits) for performance Rounding Line-level rounding, half-up, to currency minor unit; document total = sum of rounded lines (matches Avalara) Freight Taxability Per state matrix maintained by Avalara; manual override list for Manual mode  11.3 Customer / Vendor Tax Profile (new tab on both masters) Field Type Req Specification Tax Registration # (per jurisdiction) Table No State + registration/VAT number; printed on invoices where required

Field Type Req Specification Default Tax Code Dropdown No Fallback when line-level determination yields nothing Exemption Certificates Embedded view — Read-only embed of ACC 19.2 records for this customer; Add button deep-links there Use Tax Accrual (vendor) Toggle No ON: bills from this vendor with no tax charged auto-accrue use tax (DR 7400 or inventory / CR 2300-use) per ACC 19.3 Use Tax report Tax Address Role Dropdown Yes Which address drives jurisdiction: Ship-To (default) / Bill-To (services)  11.4 Nexus and Region Handling Element Specification Nexus register Grid: State, Entity, Nexus Type (Physical / Economic), Effective From, Registration #, Filing Frequency. Monthly, the Tax Reconciliation report (ACC 19.3) is reviewed; unexplained GL-vs-Avalara differences become tax adjustment JEs (approval: Controller). ▸ Manual mode: rate rows are effective-dated; edits create new rows (history immutable); changes route to Controller approval; documents always use the rate valid on their tax point date (invoice date). ▸ Retroactive policy — the rule the gap register asked for: posted documents are never restated by configuration changes. A rate/nexus/exemption change applies only to documents whose tax point date is on or after the change effective date. Corrections to already-posted documents are made exclusively by credit note + rebill (which recalculate at their own dates with reference to the original), or by a tax-only adjustment JE for remittance corrections. The configuration audit trail stores effective date, changed by, and the first document affected.

PART B ORDER MANAGEMENT (SALES) Order Management carries a transformer sale from first inquiry to shipped, invoiced and recognized revenue, and carries component/spares sales through a fast make-to-stock path. It owns the Customer Master commercial view, Quotations, Sales Orders, Deliveries & Shipments, and the handoffs: to Engineering for ETO design, to Production for order-pegged manufacturing, to Inventory for reservation/pick/pack/ship, to Accounting for invoicing (ACC 3) and revenue recognition (ACC 24), and to RMA for returns (Part A §3).

Create shipment from one or more SO lines (same ship-to, same date window). Documents produced: Bill of Lading (VICS format), Packing List (Part A §5.4), CoC, test reports bundle (FTR), country-specific export docs when applicable. POD capture field (date, name, upload) closes the delivery leg and can trigger the final milestone. PART C ENGINEERING — BOM, CAD AND CHANGE CONTROL Engineering owns the technical definition of every product: the engineering item, the Engineering BOM (EBOM), the controlled drawing set, and all change control. Production consumes a released Manufacturing BOM (MBOM) and routing; Costing rolls the MBOM; the Material Master BOM tab (INV 2.3.9) is a read-only window into what this Part produces. The design goal: an engineer can take an ETO order from Sales configuration to released drawings, EBOM and MBOM without leaving the module, and nothing unreleased can ever be bought, built or shipped.

4. Engineering Change Control (ECR / ECO) 4.1 ECR — Engineering Change Request Screen ID ENG-ECR-001 URL Pattern /engineering/change-requests Purpose Anyone proposes a change; engineering evaluates impact; CCB decides  Field Type Req Specification ECR # Auto Auto ECR-{YYYY}-{SEQ:4} Requested By / Source User + dropdown Yes Source: Engineering, Production, Quality (NCR link), Purchasing (obsolescence/AVL), Sales (customer change), RMA/Field Failure Affected Objects Multi-lookup Yes Items, EBOMs/MBOMs, drawings, routings; validated against Where-Used Change Description / Reason Textareas Yes What and why; classification dropdown: Safety/Regulatory, Cost Reduction, Quality/CAPA, Supply Continuity, Customer Request, Documentation Only Impact Analysis Panel Auto + manual Auto System computes and displays: open WOs using affected revisions (count + list), on-hand stock of affected components (qty + value), open POs (qty + value), open SO lines, cost delta (current rollup vs simulated rollup with proposed change — one-click simulation via costing engine Part I §3), affected customers (designs shipped). Engineer adds narrative + risk assessment Priority Dropdown Yes Emergency (safety — 24h CCB SLA), Urgent, Standard CCB Decision Panel Auto Change Control Board = Engineering Manager + Quality Manager + Production Manager + (cost delta > $10k) Controller. Votes recorded; outcome Approve → spawns ECO; Reject / Defer with reason  4.2 ECO — Engineering Change Order Field / Panel Specification ECO #, ECR link ECO-{YYYY}-{SEQ:4}; one ECO may implement several ECRs Task Checklist Generated from affected objects: revise EBOM X to Rev n+1, revise drawing DOC-…, update routing, update inspection plan, run costing simulation, update MBOM, notify purchasing, customer re-approval (when the design was customer-approved — mandatory task auto-added). Each task has owner + due date + status; ECO cannot close with open tasks Effectivity Decision §4.5 — date, serial, or immediate Old-Stock Disposition Mandatory decision per affected component with on-hand > 0: Use-Up (consume remaining then switch — system watches stock and flips revision automatically at zero), Rework (creates RWO), Scrap (posts 5320 with approval per INV thresholds), Return to Vendor (RTV) Open-WO Disposition Per open WO on old revision: Complete As-Is / Incorporate Change (WO gets change task + updated pick list deltas) / Stop & Rework Release Action Atomic cutover: all new revisions (EBOM, MBOM, drawings, routing, plans) release together at effectivity; partial release impossible

6. EBOM → MBOM Transformation The MBOM is what Production and Costing consume (the BOM-TX-… objects already referenced by INV 2.3.9). Transformation is a guided screen, not a copy-paste: 1. Source: a Released EBOM revision. Target: MBOM for a specific plant (multi-plant ready; single plant today). 2. Phantom resolution preview: phantoms flatten into parent per policy; engineer confirms. 3. Operation mapping: every line’s Consume At Operation validated against the chosen routing (Part D §4); unmapped lines listed and must be resolved. 4. Packaging & consumables append: MBOM adds manufacturing-only items (crating per packing spec, oil top-up allowance, welding consumables) that the EBOM deliberately excludes; these lines are flagged MFG-Add and never sync back to the EBOM. 5. Scrap % review: costing-relevant; defaults from EBOM, production may adjust with Engineering approval. 6. Release: MBOM status Active; prior Active revision → Superseded; cost rollup invalidation flag set for the parent (Part I §3 picks it up in the next costing run); Material Master BOM tab now renders this revision. Dev Note: Sync guard: if the source EBOM gets a newer released revision, all MBOMs derived from older revisions show an “EBOM ahead” banner and appear in the Engineering exceptions panel until an ECO task regenerates them. MBOMs are never edited structurally outside this transformation — structural change always starts at the EBOM (single source of truth).

PART D PRODUCTION MANAGEMENT Production Management plans, releases, executes, confirms and settles all manufacturing. It consumes released MBOMs and routings (Part C), reserves and draws materials from Inventory, employs Quality gates at defined operations, generates the serialized genealogy that traceability depends on, and settles every order’s cost to the GL with variances split by cause (Part A §8). This Part also specifies the physical flow of a transformer through the plant and the exact screen the operator touches at each workstation.

1. Plant Model — Work Centers and Floor Flow 1.1 Standard Work Center Set (Transformer Plant) Work centers are the scheduling and costing resources. Initial set for the PE plant — each row becomes a Work Center master record (§2); the development partner must treat this as seed data, not hard-coding: WC Code Work Center Function Cost Center WC-050 Tank Fabrication & Welding Steel cutting, forming, welding, pressure test of tanks, radiators fit-up CC-1150 WC-100 Core Cutting CRGO slitting, cropping, mitred cutting (step-lap) CC-1100 WC-110 Core Stacking Lamination stacking, core clamping, core loss pre-check CC-1100 WC-200 LV Winding Foil/strip LV coils CC-1200 WC-210 HV Winding Wire/CTC HV coils, layer/disc winding CC-1200 WC-220 Coil Assembly & Sizing Phase assembly, sizing press, brazing CC-1200 WC-230 Vapor Phase Drying / Oven Insulation drying cycles (batch resource) CC-1250 WC-300 Core & Coil Assembly Core-coil marriage, cleating, lead dressing, tap lead connection CC-1300 WC-310 Tanking Active part into tank, gasketing, cover fit CC-1300 WC-320 Vacuum & Oil Filling Vacuum cycle, degassed oil fill, standing time CC-1300 WC-400 Final Assembly & Fittings Bushings, tap changer, radiators, conservator, cabinet, wiring, accessories CC-1400 WC-500 Test Lab Routine tests per IEEE C57.12.90; type tests when ordered CC-1500 (Quality) WC-600 Paint & Finishing Blast, prime, topcoat, nameplate mounting CC-1600 WC-700 Packing & Shipping Prep Drain/gas-fill for transport, accessory crating, loading CC-1700  1.2 Product Flow Through the Floor Reference flow for a padmount/power transformer; the routing (§4) encodes this per design. Parallel branches: the tank (WC-050) and the active part (WC-100→WC-300) progress concurrently and merge at Tanking. Every operation start/complete is a barcode scan event (Part F, SP-10/SP-11); every material issue is a scan (SP-08/SP-09); every QC gate is an inspection record. 1. Core: WC-100 → WC-110: CRGO batches issued by heat number (scan captures batch → serial genealogy); stacked core weighed and core loss pre-checked (in-process inspection IP-INP “Core Assembly”). 2. Windings: WC-200 / WC-210 draw copper (batch-scanned, conductivity certs on file); winding data sheet (turns, tap positions) from drawing set on-screen; QC gate “Winding” (turn count, resistance, dimensions). 3. Coil assembly & drying: WC-220 assembles phases; WC-230 dries per cycle recipe — batch resource: multiple orders share an oven run (the terminal supports multi-order batch confirmation with cycle chart attachment). 4. Tank line: WC-050 fabricates/receives tank; pressure/leak test recorded as inspection; blast & prime may run at WC-600 before assembly per routing variant. 

=== PAGE 70 ===
ENTERPRISE ERP — UNIFIED BUILD SPECIFICATION  |  V1.0 CONFIDENTIAL 
CONFIDENTIAL Page 70 of 312 
5. Assembly merge: WC-300 core & coil into tank at WC-310; WC-320 vacuum + oil fill (oil batch scanned — traceability to oil supplier batch); QC gate “Pre-Test” checklist. 6. Final assembly WC-400: serialized components installed and scan-linked as child serials (bushings, tap changer, relays — SP-12 parent-child link scan). Accessory checklist from SO configuration. 7. Test WC-500: Final Test per plan IP-FIN (Part E §4); FTR generated; failures route to Rework (§9). 8. Finish WC-600 → WC-700: paint, nameplate (nameplate data printed from system — SP-13 verification scan of nameplate serial barcode), pack per Part A §5, FG receipt (§8) or direct staging to shipment.

3. MRP Workbench & Planned Orders Screen ID PP-MRP-001 URL Pattern /production/mrp Purpose Net-requirements planning across all demand and supply; the planner’s daily control desk. Extends the read-only INV 15.9 view into the acting workbench  3.1 MRP Run Element Specification Scope & schedule Nightly full run + on-demand run (item / item group / plant); regenerative (full re-plan) with net-change option Demand inputs Confirmed SO lines (MTS ship dates), WO component requirements, forecast (INV 15.8, consumed by actuals per period), safety stock floors (Part A §10), quarantine exclusions Supply inputs On-hand available by warehouse, open POs (confirmed dates), open WOs (scheduled completion), planned orders, open transfers, subcontract POs Logic Low-level-code explosion through MBOMs with scrap %; lot sizing per item (Lot-for-Lot default; Fixed Qty; Min/Multiple rounding); lead-time offsetting (purchased: vendor lead time + GR processing days; made: routing-based lead time); make/buy split by item flag Outputs Planned orders (PLO- for make, PR proposals for buy), reschedule-in/out messages for existing supply, exception messages  3.2 Exception Messages Code Message Suggested Action Button EX-01 Shortage — no supply covers requirement on date Create PR / Create WO EX-02 Reschedule In — supply later than need Expedite (writes to PO Expedite Board, Part G §7) EX-03 Reschedule Out — supply earlier than need Push out (PO change proposal / WO reschedule) EX-04 Excess — supply exceeds demand horizon Cancel proposal EX-05 SS-BREACH — plan dips below safety stock Raise supply proposal per Part A §10.2 EX-06 Past-due supply Expedite / re-date EX-07 BOM/routing missing or unreleased for make item Open Engineering exception  3.3 Planned Order Conversion Planner selects planned orders → Convert: make → Production Order draft (WO) with pegs preserved; buy → PR (Part G §3) grouped by vendor per lot-sizing. Mass-convert with preview; conversions log the MRP run ID for audit. Firmed planned orders (planner locks date/qty) survive the next regenerative run.

8. Production Receipt & Order Settlement 8.1 FG Receipt Final operation completion (after Final Test pass and paint/pack ops per routing) enables Receive to Stock: a Check-In of type Production Receipt (INV 5.1) auto-filled with WO, item, serial, qty good. Location: FG warehouse or STAGE-OUT for direct-ship pegged orders (auto-reservation to the SO line either way). Accounting Entry — Production Receipt (FG at standard) Debit Credit Amount Basis Notes 1330 Finished Goods 1320 WIP Good Qty × released standard cost of the FG item Profit center: product line. Matches INV Sec 12 #6  8.2 Settlement (at Close/TECO) Close is allowed when: all ops confirmed or cancelled, all inspections closed, FG received or scrap-disposed. The settlement run (per order, or batch month-end run screen PP-SETTLE-001 with preview) clears residual WIP to variance accounts split by cause: Accounting Entry — Order Settlement — variance split Debit Credit Amount Basis Notes 5220 Material Usage Variance 1320 WIP Actual material − standard material for good output Unfavorable shown; favorable posts as credit. CC: production cost center of primary WC; PC: product line 5230 Labor Rate Variance 1320 WIP (Actual rate − std rate) × actual hours Same dimensions 5240 Labor Efficiency Variance 1320 WIP (Actual hours − std hours) × std rate Same 5250 Overhead Absorption Variance 1320 WIP Applied − actual overhead share Same  Dev Note: After settlement the order’s WIP balance is exactly zero by construction; the settlement document stores the variance math per Part A §8 formulas and links each amount to its JE. Reopening a settled order requires Controller and reverses the settlement JE first.

PART E QUALITY MANAGEMENT Quality Management is promoted to a top-level module unifying the screens already specified in INV (Incoming Inspection 6.1, Stock Hold/Release 6.2, In-Process 15.2, Outgoing/Pre-Shipment 15.3, NCR 15.4, CoC 15.5) with the pieces this volume adds: Inspection Plans (Part A §6), Final Test, CAPA as a standalone register, Supplier Corrective Action (SCAR), Calibration, and the Quality dashboard. The through-line: every inspection everywhere executes from an Inspection Plan, every failure can escalate to NCR → CAPA/SCAR, and every quality event is permanently attached to the batch/serial it concerns.

PART F BARCODING & TRACEABILITY Barcoding is the data-capture backbone: every physical movement, consumption, build step and quality event is confirmed by scan, which is what makes the Material Traceability Report (INV 10.4) trustworthy end to end. This Part specifies symbologies, label formats, hardware, the complete scan-point matrix (which step scans what, validates what, and posts what), error behavior, and the genealogy data model.

Barcode format default per INV 11.1 policy remains Code128; this Part fixes the content standards.

2. Hardware & Printing Component Requirement Printers ZPL-compatible thermal transfer (Zebra ZT411 class) at Receiving, Quarantine, Pack, Serial/nameplate station; laser for travelers. Print service: server-rendered ZPL templates, printer registry per station, reprint with reason + audit (reprints watermark “DUPLICATE” except location labels) Scanners Rugged 2D imagers (handheld + hands-free at fixed stations); keyboard-wedge over WebSocket wedge service so the web terminal receives scans as events, not keystrokes into random fields (focus-lock pattern) Mobile Android rugged devices running the responsive web app for count entry, putaway, picking; camera fallback scanning permitted for supervisors only (slower, logged) Offline Terminals queue scan events locally for up to 4 hours of network loss; queued events post in order with original timestamps; conflicts (e.g. stock moved meanwhile) land in a Scan Exception queue for supervisor resolution — never silently dropped

4. Error Handling & Governance Rule Specification Wrong-scan messaging Every failed validation shows: what was scanned, what was expected, E-code, and the next action (“Scan a component listed for op 30” ) — with audible + color feedback on device Override discipline Overridable validations (batch suggestion, staging shortcuts) require supervisor badge scan inline; hard validations (wrong item, wrong serial, cal expired, HU not on shipment) have no override — the correcting transaction must be done properly Label reprint Any label reprint requires reason code; serial/nameplate reprints additionally require Quality approval (duplicate nameplate risk) Unlabeled stock exception Found stock without label: supervisor flow “Identify & Relabel” — resolves against expected inventory at that bin, prints label, logs exception; feeds count accuracy metrics Scan telemetry All scan events (incl. failures) stored 24 months: device, user, ts, payload, result — the forensic layer beneath the audit trail

5. Genealogy Data Model & Traceability Output Entity Key Fields Notes genealogy_event event_id, type (ISSUE/INSTALL/TEST/PACK/SHIP/RETURN), wo_id, op_id, parent_serial_id, child_serial_id, batch_id, item_id, qty, operator_id, station, ts, source_scan_id Append-only; one row per SP-08/11/12/13/14/16/18/19 event with stock impact or linkage serial_link parent_serial_id, child_serial_id, installed_event_id, removed_event_id Current + historical structure (removal during rework recorded, not overwritten) batch_consumption wo_id, op_id, batch_id, qty, event_id Aggregated view for the traceability tree  The Material Traceability Report (INV 10.4) is extended to render from these tables: input a FG serial → tree of ops with consumed batches (vendor, PO, QI result, certs), child serials, operators and inspections per node, forward to customer/site/warranty; input a raw batch (e.g. suspect CRGO heat) → every WO, serial, customer, and shipped location it reached, with one-click customer notification list export. PART G PROCUREMENT & PURCHASING Purchasing turns demand (MRP proposals, reorder triggers, manual requests) into on-time, on-spec, on-price supply. It owns the Vendor Master operational view, Approved Vendor Lists, Purchase Requisitions, RFQs, Purchase Orders (including subcontract), confirmations and expediting. Receiving (GRN), three-way match and payment are already specified (INV 5.6, ACC 6/8/25); vendor quality feedback loops in from Part E. 

=== PAGE 100 ===
ENTERPRISE ERP — UNIFIED BUILD SPECIFICATION  |  V1.0 CONFIDENTIAL 
CONFIDENTIAL Page 100 of 312 
1. How Purchasing Orders Parts and Materials — The Daily Operating Loop 1. Demand intake. The buyer’s workbench opens on: PR queue (MRP-generated + reorder auto-PRs + manual), MRP exception messages assigned to buy items (EX-01/02/03/06), and the Expedite Board. Everything is bucketed by buyer code (items carry a Buyer assignment). 2. Source determination. For each PR line the system proposes the source: AVL preferred vendor → last PO price and lead time → open contract/blanket if one exists. Buyer accepts or overrides (override reason logged). No AVL vendor for a QI-critical item = hard block (E-PUR-02) → start AVL qualification or RFQ. 3. RFQ when needed. New items, expiring price agreements, or value > $25k without contract (policy) route through RFQ (§4): multi-vendor invite, sealed responses, comparison grid, award writes the new AVL price basis. 4. PO creation. PRs group into POs per vendor per lot-sizing (one PO, many lines; delivery schedules per line where staged deliveries are wanted). Approval workflow by value (ACC 22 engine): Buyer ≤ $25k, Purchasing Manager ≤ $250k, + Controller above, + CFO above $1M. 5. Transmission & confirmation. PO PDF (entity letterhead, terms, quality clauses, drawing attachments for fabricated parts) emails to vendor; vendor confirmation (date + qty per line) is captured — unconfirmed POs older than 3 business days surface on the workbench. 6. Expedite / de-expedite. MRP reschedule messages write suggestions onto the Expedite Board (§7); buyer actions become dated change requests to the vendor with full history. 7. Receipt & closure. GRN against PO (INV 5.1/5.6), QI per plan, three-way match (ACC 6), PPV/IPV visible in CVA (Part A §8). Lines close at full receipt + invoice; short-close with reason allowed (Purchasing Manager).

PART H COST CENTERS & PROFIT CENTERS (CONTROLLING) Controlling gives every dollar in the GL two management dimensions: where it was incurred (cost center — responsibility for spend) and which business it belongs to (profit center — responsibility for margin). ACC Section 10 already carries the dimensions on journal items; this Part supplies what was still missing per the accounting gap analysis: the masters, the hierarchies, the sources and derivation rules, allocations, and the reports — and wires the dimensions into every logistics and production posting specified in this volume. 

=== PAGE 109 ===
ENTERPRISE ERP — UNIFIED BUILD SPECIFICATION  |  V1.0 CONFIDENTIAL 
CONFIDENTIAL Page 109 of 312 
1. Sources — Where Cost Centers and Profit Centers Come From Dimension Source of Truth Governance Cost Centers The organizational chart: one cost center per department or supervisory area that owns budgetary responsibility. Seeded from the org structure workshop with Finance; created/changed only by Controlling (Controller approval), effective-dated, never deleted (deactivated) 
Annual review with budget cycle (ACC 21); mid-year changes via change request with mapping rules for history Profit Centers The management view of the business: product lines and service lines per legal entity, defined by the CFO. Stable small set; changes are rare and CFO-approved P&L segmentation must reconcile to entity totals — every P&L-relevant posting carries exactly one PC  1.1 Seed Cost Center Hierarchy (PE Transformer plant) Node Cost Center Contains / Maps To CC-1000 Manufacturing summary node All production cost centers — CC-1100 Core Shop postable WC-100, WC-110 — CC-1150 Fabrication postable WC-050 — CC-1200 Winding postable WC-200, WC-210, WC-220 — CC-1250 Drying postable WC-230 — CC-1300 Assembly postable WC-300, WC-310, WC-320 — CC-1400 Final Assembly postable WC-400 — CC-1600 Paint & Finish postable WC-600 — CC-1700 Pack & Ship postable WC-700 + shipping dock CC-1500 Quality postable WC-500 Test Lab + inspectors + calibration CC-2000 Warehousing postable Warehouses (each warehouse master links here — INV 3.2 Cost Center field) CC-3000 Engineering postable Design engineering CC-4000 Purchasing & Materials postable Buyers, planners CC-5000 Sales & Customer Service postable — CC-9000 G&A summary CC-9100 Executive, CC-9200 Finance, CC-9300 IT, CC-9400 HR, CC-9500 Facilities  1.2 Seed Profit Center Hierarchy Profit Center Scope PC-100 Distribution Transformers Pad-mount & pole-type product families PC-200 Power Transformers Medium/large power units

3. Derivation Rules — How Every Posting Gets Its Dimensions Priority-ordered engine, evaluated at posting time by the shared Posting Service; the first rule that yields a value wins per dimension. Every financially relevant document in all three volumes passes through it. Rule evaluations are stored on the journal item (rule # that fired) for auditability. # Rule Cost Center Profit Center 1 Explicit entry on the document line (role-permitted) As entered As entered 2 Production confirmation / WO posting Work center → CC link (Part D §2) Parent item product line 3 Inventory movement (receipt, issue, transfer, adjustment, count, scrap) Warehouse master CC (INV 3.2) Item product line (item master PC field, new — added to INV 2.3.3 B GL section) 4 Sales documents (PGI/COGS, revenue, freight out, RMA credit) — (revenue/COGS carry PC only; freight/warranty expense: shipping/quality CC) SO line item product line; header PC for order-level charges 5 Purchasing expense lines (MRO/services) PR/PO line cost center (mandatory, Part G §3) CC default PC 6 Expense JE with CC only — CC master Default Profit Center 7 Fallback CC-9000 G&A + exception report row PC-900 + exception report row  Dev Note: Validation posture: for P&L accounts both dimensions are mandatory at posting (rule 7 guarantees a value but writes the posting to the CO Exception Report — target zero rows). Balance sheet accounts carry PC where meaningful (inventory, WIP by product line) and skip CC except where specified. The Posting Service exposes a simulation endpoint so any screen can preview derived dimensions before posting — surfaced as the “GL Preview” panel already specified on Stock Adjustments (INV 5.8) and extended to all posting screens.

PART I COSTING & PRICING Costing computes what things cost — standards built from MBOM + routing + rates, WIP and settlement math, and the estimate engine that prices ETO quotes before a design exists. Pricing computes what things sell for — price lists, agreements, commodity surcharges and margin control. The two meet in the margin floor: no quote or price master survives approval below the floor without the defined escalation.

2. Work Center Rates Screen ID CST-RATE-001 URL Pattern /costing/work-center-rates Purpose Annual (and interim) computation and release of labor/machine/overhead rates per work center  Element Specification Rate math Labor rate = planned cost-center labor cost ÷ planned productive hours (from capacity model × efficiency). Overhead rate = planned CC overhead pool (incl. received allocations, Part H §5) ÷ same hours. Machine rate where configured = machine pool ÷ machine hours Screen Grid per WC: planned costs (pull from approved budget, ACC 21, by CC), planned hours (from WC capacity), computed rates, prior-year rates, delta %; simulation column (what-if hours/costs); Release action versions the rates with effective date Governance Rates released annually with the budget; interim change requires Controller approval and triggers costing-run guidance (mid-year rate changes revalue nothing retroactively — they apply to confirmations from effective date; standards keep the rate frozen at last rollup) Feedback CO-R3 over/under-absorption report is the annual sanity check on rate quality

3. Cost Estimates & Costing Runs 3.1 Cost Estimate (single item / quote support) Screen ID CST-CE-001 URL Pattern /costing/estimates Purpose Cost one item or one ETO configuration: current-standard simulation, what-if, and quote-backing estimates (CE-# links on quotes, Part B §3.2)  Element Specification Basis selector Released MBOM+routing (standard simulation) / Draft EBOM (engineering what-if) / Parametric ETO model: for quotes before design, estimator enters driver quantities — core weight (kg), copper weight (kg), oil volume (gal), tank steel weight, bushing/tap changer selections, est. labor hours per WC — against a maintained driver-rate table (e.g. $/kg CRGO at current commodity basis); template per product family seeded from historical actuals Itemization view Cost tree: every component and operation with qty, rate, value; component prices show source (standard / AVL basis / manual override with note); subtotals by cost component Comparison Estimate vs current standard vs last actual (settled WOs of same/similar design) side by side Status & reuse Draft → Released (locks values, quotable) → Superseded; quote margin panel consumes Released estimates only  3.2 Costing Run (mass standard rollup) Screen ID CST-RUN-001 URL Pattern /costing/runs Purpose Periodic (annual + on-demand) recalculation and release of standard costs across the item base — the engine behind Standard Cost Maintenance (INV 8.2), which remains the approval/impact UI  1. Scope: plant, item selection (all standard-cost items / item groups / changed-BOM-flag only — the invalidation flags set by MBOM releases, Part C §6). 2. Explode & roll: low-level-code order; errors collected, not fatal (missing rate, missing AVL basis, BOM loop) into an error list with owners; items with errors are excluded from release. 3. Review: per item old standard vs proposed, delta %, value impact on current on-hand (qty × delta); sort by absolute impact; drill to itemization. 4. Mark & release: two-step per INV 8.2 (mark = staged, release = effective). Release posts revaluation per INV Sec 12 #15 (1310/1330 vs 5330) and stamps the new standard + effective date on items; PPV baselines shift from that moment. 5. Frequency policy: full run annually at budget; interim runs for new items weekly (auto, new-items-only scope); ad-hoc runs Controller-approved.

4. WIP Accounting & Settlement Math (Worked Example) One place to see the whole cost flow of a production order, consistent with Part D §6.1/§8 and Part A §8. Example: WO-2026-00266, one 1500 kVA padmount, standard cost $61,000 (material 42,000 / labor 8,000 / overhead 11,000). Event DR CR Amount Issue CRGO, copper, oil, components (actual qty) 1320 WIP 1310 RM $43,150 Labor confirmations 410 h @ std $48 1320 WIP 7500 absorption (CC credits) $19,680 → of which labor $ per rate split; overhead applied same event Overhead applied 410 h @ $27 1320 WIP 7500 absorption $11,070 Scrap at winding (damaged copper) 5320 Scrap 1320 WIP $600 FG receipt at standard 1330 FG 1320 WIP $61,000 Settlement — MUV (43,150 − 42,000 = 1,150 unfav) 5220 1320 $1,150 Settlement — labor+OH variance (30,750 applied? vs std 19,000) split LRV/LEV/OAV per formulas 5230/5240/5250 1320 $11,150 total, split per Part A §8 Result — — WIP balance for the order = exactly $0  Dev Note: Numbers are illustrative; the settlement engine must reproduce the invariant, not the example: FG receipt at standard + variances posted = total actual charges − scrap already expensed, leaving order WIP at zero. The 7500 absorption credits sit on the sending cost centers and are the counterweight measured by CO-R3 over/under absorption.

5. Actual Costing Note (Scope Boundary) The system is standard-cost primary for make items with full variance capture; moving average remains available for purchased items per INV valuation methods. A parallel actual-costing ledger (periodic actual cost per unit) is explicitly out of Phase 1–2 scope; the data to enable it later (actual charges per order, per-batch receipt costs) is already captured, so no schema change is expected.

3. Implementation Roadmap (This Volume) Sequenced to extend the inventory build (INV Section 13 phases, weeks 1–12) and the accounting plan (ACC Section 27, 24 weeks). Dependencies: Controlling masters precede any posting-bearing module here; Engineering precedes Production; Production precedes full Costing; the nine gap items ride with their host modules. 1.	Purpose,	Scope	and	Position	in	the	Document	Set This volume is the definitive, field-by-field implementation specification for Material Management in the PE Transformer ERP. It is the fourth controlled volume of the build package and is written to the same standard, structure and depth as the three volumes already in the development partner’s hands: Volume Covers Status Inventory & Warehouse Module Spec V3.0 Warehousing, stock transactions, serial/batch tracking, counting, valuation screens, inventory GL map. Owns the Material Master record physically (screens INV 2.2/2.3). Released — build in progress Accounting Module Implementation Spec V2.0 AR, AP, GL, bank, tax engine, intercompany, budgeting, workflows, RBAC, ASC 606, period close. Released Manufacturing Core Spec V2.0 Order Management, Engineering & BOM, Production, Quality, Barcoding & Traceability, Procurement, RMA, Controlling, Costing & Pricing, plus the nine gap-register closures. Released 
This Book — Material Management The material object itself and every contract it has with the rest of the system: material types and numbering, all Material Master views to field level, creation and change governance, valuation and automatic account determination, purchasing info records and source lists, planning parameters, special stock ownership, batch/serial profiles, mass maintenance, migration and data quality. 
This document 
1.1	Why	a	Separate	Material	Management	Volume	Exists The material hub principle is stated in Book I (MFG Core §2.3, the Material Master is the hub), and the hub itself must be specified as a buildable object. Attributes were defined where each module needed them: the Sales tab in MFG Core Part A §4, purchasing data in Part G, planning data split between INV 2.2 and Part D, valuation data in INV 2.3.3. What was missing, and what this volume supplies, is: • A single material type model with a behavior matrix that tells the development team exactly which views, validations, postings and defaults apply to each kind of material PE handles, from CRGO coils to nameplates to transformer oil to MRO consumables to service items. • The complete Material Master screen specification: every view, every field, every validation, every button, ownership and edit rights per field group, and what each downstream module reads from it. 

=== PAGE 135 ===
ENTERPRISE ERP — UNIFIED BUILD SPECIFICATION  |  V1.0 CONFIDENTIAL 
CONFIDENTIAL Page 135 of 312 
• Governed lifecycles: how a material comes into existence (including the engineering placeholder path already sketched in MFG Core Part C §3.2), which departments must complete which data before it can be used, how it is changed under control, and how it is retired without stranding stock, open orders or history. • Automatic account determination: the deterministic matrix that turns any stock-relevant transaction into GL postings using valuation class and event keys, replacing the per-screen posting notes with one engine specification. • Purchasing master data in full: vendor-material info records, source lists and their interaction with AVL, MRP source determination and PO pricing. • Special stock and ownership types: vendor consignment, customer property, subcontract stock, sales-order-pegged stock and in-transit stock, each with quantity, value and posting treatment. • Data governance, mass maintenance, migration and completeness measurement, covering data governance, migration and the master ownership register. Build Note: Nothing in this volume moves a screen out of INV, ACC or MFG Core. Where an existing screen gains fields or behavior, the change is specified here as a delta and flagged “DELTA to INV x.x” or “DELTA to MFG x”. Where an existing specification already covers a view in full (for example the Sales tab, MFG Core Part A §4), this volume references it and specifies only what is new. Build against all four volumes together. 1.2	Enterprise	Requirements	Addressed	by	This	Book The following enterprise requirements are specified or advanced in this Book. Finite scheduling, CAD and PLM connectors, intercompany, platform architecture, testing and cutover are owned by Book III and Book IV. Ref Requirement Specified In F-04 (partial) Plant-specific master data readiness for multi-plant manufacturing Section 3.3 (plant-level data split), Section 18 (material_plant_data entity) F-11 (partial) Consignment/VMI foundations within advanced procurement Section 14.2 vendor consignment, Section 7.4 source list T-01 (partial) Physical data model for master data: keys, effectivity, audit Section 18 with constraints, effectivity and audit rules for all MM entities T-10 Master-data stewardship, quality rules, lineage, retention Part F: governance roles, completeness KPIs, change logging, archive rules D-01 Cross-volume master ownership register Section 3.2 field-group ownership matrix, Section 6–13 linkage contracts D-04 (partial) Migration: source inventory, mapping, cleansing, mock loads Section 16.3 migration specification for material, batch, serial and info-record data 1.3	Reading	Conventions • Screen IDs follow the established pattern with the module prefix MM (MM-MAT-001, MM-MCR-001, MM-IR-001, MM-SL-001, MM-VAL-001). Existing screens keep their INV/MFG IDs. • Field tables use the columns Field / Type / Req / Specification. “Cond.” means conditionally required and the condition is stated in the specification column. • GL posting tables use the chart of accounts already defined in INV 11.4, ACC Section 13 and MFG Core §2.4. New accounts introduced by this volume are listed in Section 2.6 and follow

the same numbering logic; every posting rule references mapping keys, never hard-coded numbers. • Every listing screen inherits the standard table pattern and every create screen inherits the standard layout exactly as established in INV Section 2 and ACC Section 2. These global behaviors are not repeated per screen. • The abbreviations INV, ACC and MFG refer to the three companion volumes with their section numbers.

PART	A MATERIAL	MODEL	AND	MASTER	DATA Part A defines what a material is in this system: the type model that drives all behavior, the numbering and classification schemes, the unit-of-measure model, and the complete view-by-view Material Master specification. 
2.	Material	Type	Model 2.1	Material	Types Every material carries exactly one Material Type, assigned at creation and immutable afterwards (conversion is a controlled supersession, §4.6, never an edit). The type decides which Material Master views exist, which defaults apply, whether the material is inventory-managed and valuated, and which valuation classes are allowed. The current item categories in INV 2.2 map onto these types one-to-one at migration (Section 16.3). Type Code Typical PE Examples Key Behavior Raw Material RAW CRGO coil by heat, copper wire/CTC/foil, insulation kraft, tank steel plate, transformer oil in bulk Inventory-managed, valuated (1310 range), batch-managed by default, purchased only (no BOM as parent), QI-required by default Semi-Finished SEMI Wound coils, stacked cores, fabricated tanks, sub-assemblies Inventory-managed, valuated (1320/1310 per policy), made in-house, must carry released EBOM/MBOM and routing before use, serial or batch per profile Finished Good FIN Padmount transformers, power transformers, catalog distribution units Inventory-managed, valuated (1330), sellable default ON, serialized default ON, standard cost mandatory before first receipt Trading Good TRADE PE Grid Components merchandise: bushings, arresters, connectors bought and resold Inventory-managed, valuated (1330 or 1310 per mapping), purchased and sellable, no BOM as parent Packaging PACK Crates, skids, oil totes, desiccant, VCI wrap Inventory-managed, valuated (1360), consumed by backflush at HU close (MFG Part A §5.4) Spare Part SPARE Service spares for field and warranty work: gaskets, gauges, valves, radiator fins Inventory-managed, valuated, sellable optional, links to service BOMs (Phase 2 field reserved) Consumable / MRO CONS Welding wire, gloves, blades, shop supplies Inventory-managed optional (config per material), expensed at receipt or issue to cost center; no product costing relevance Service SERV Testing services, subcontract operations, freight, engineering services Never inventory-managed, never valuated; PO lines post to expense or WIP per context; sellable for service revenue (4150) Non-Stock NSTK One-time buys direct to a WO or cost center, customer-supplied reference items No stock record; quantity tracked on the consuming document only; expensed on receipt 2.2	Type	Behavior	Matrix
