Material Type Format Example NSTK NS-{SEQ:5} NS-00202 • The {GROUP} token is the first-level material group code (§2.4), fixed at creation. Renumbering is prohibited; if the group was wrong the material is superseded (§4.6). • Manual numbers are not permitted. The Engineering Placeholder path (§4.3) reserves the number at placeholder creation so drawings and BOM lines never reference a temporary code. • Material numbers never encode revision. Revision-controlled materials (fabricated parts to drawing) carry revision on the linked document set and BOM effectivity, per MFG Part C. 2.4	Material	Groups	and	Classification Material Groups are a three-level hierarchy used for search, reporting, approval routing, material overhead rates (MFG Part I §1) and default assignment of valuation class, buyer and inspection plans. The hierarchy is maintained in Configuration → Material Settings → Material Groups (Materials Manager approval; effective-dated; nodes deactivate, never delete). Level 1 (examples) Level 2 (examples) Level 3 (examples) CRG Core Steel CRGO Standard Grades, CRGO HiB M-4, M-5, 23ZDKH85 CU Copper & Conductors Wire, CTC, Foil, Busbar By gauge/spec family INS Insulation Kraft/Crepe, Pressboard, DDP, Resins By spec OIL Fluids Mineral Oil, Ester Fluid By standard (D3487 Type II…) STL Steel & Fabrication Plate, Structural, Hardware By grade/size family BSH Components Bushings, Tap Changers, Gauges, Relays, Radiators By voltage class / vendor family PKG Packaging Crates, Totes, Dunnage — MRO Consumables Weld, PPE, Shop — In addition to the group hierarchy, each material carries the classification attributes needed for compliance and trade: HTS/HS code (customs), Schedule B (export), country of origin default (overridden by batch actuals at receipt), Buy America relevance flag (feeds MFG Part G §5.1), and hazardous material profile (UN number, packing group, hazard class) used by Packing (MFG Part A §5) and shipping documents. A free characteristic panel (typed key-value attributes defined per material group, e.g. voltage class, kVA, conductor gauge) supports parametric search; characteristics are search and print data only in Phase 1 and become configuration model inputs in the product-configuration phase flagged by the review (F-02). 2.5	Units	of	Measure The UoM model extends INV 2.6 and is restated here as the binding contract because every module depends on it: • Base UoM: the unit in which stock quantities and valuation are kept. Immutable after the first stock-relevant posting (hard rule; enforced at save, E-MM-04). • Alternative UoMs with fixed conversion factors: Purchase UoM (per vendor overridable in the info record, §7.2), Sales UoM (MFG Part A §4.1), Issue UoM (shop-floor convenience, e.g. copper issued by kg while stocked by kg but bought by lb), and up to 6 additional conversions. Every conversion is a rational pair (numerator/denominator) to avoid floating drift: 1 lb = 45359237/100000000 kg is stored, not 0.45359237. 

=== PAGE 140 ===
ENTERPRISE ERP — UNIFIED BUILD SPECIFICATION  |  V1.0 CONFIDENTIAL 
CONFIDENTIAL Page 140 of 312 
• Dimension classes (mass, length, volume, count, area) prevent nonsense conversions; cross-dimension conversions (e.g. length of conductor to mass) are permitted only via a material-specific conversion with both UoMs declared on the material and an engineering-approved factor. • Catch weight is out of scope; oil is bought and stocked by volume (gal) with density held as a characteristic for freight calculations. 2.6	New	GL	Accounts	Introduced	by	This	Volume These accounts extend GL Account Mapping (INV 11.4) and the CoA (ACC Section 13). As with the MFG Core additions, the numbers are proposals consistent with existing ranges; the Controller confirms or remaps them once in GL Account Mapping, and every posting rule references mapping keys. Path Initiator Notes Migration Data load (Section 16.3) MCRs auto-approved by load policy but completeness KPIs still computed The MCR carries a task checklist generated from the material type. Each task = one view to complete + its owning role + SLA. Default matrix: Task (view to complete) Owner RAW SEMI/FIN TRADE PACK/CONS SERV General + classification + UoM Master Data Steward Yes Yes Yes Yes Yes Planning (MRP) Production Planner Yes Yes Yes Yes No Purchasing + first Info Record Buyer Yes If buy-complete Yes Yes Yes Production Manufacturing Engineer No Yes No No No Warehouse Warehouse Manager Yes Yes Yes Yes No Quality (+ inspection plan check) Quality Engineer Yes Yes Config No No Accounting & Costing (valuation class, method, PC) Cost Accountant Yes Yes Yes Yes Yes (rates) Sales (when sellable) Sales Admin No FIN yes Yes No Yes • Tasks run in parallel except Accounting & Costing, which requires Planning and Purchasing done first (valuation method and first price basis depend on them). • SLA default 2 business days per task; breach escalates to the role’s manager and appears on the Data Quality dashboard (§16.4). • Activation: when all tasks complete, the Materials Manager reviews the assembled record and Activates (runs V-MM-01). Rejection returns to the responsible task with comments. • The MCR stores the originating demand (EBOM line, PR, quote) and unblocks it automatically at activation: the EBOM placeholder flag clears, the PR becomes sourceable, the quote line resolves. Build Note: Do not implement per-department “mini masters”. There is one material record; the MCR only orchestrates who fills which view and in what order, with field-group edit rights per §3.2 enforced the same during and after creation. 4.3	Engineering	Placeholder	Path	—	Exact	Behavior • Create Placeholder Part (in the EBOM editor) captures: description, material group, UoM, est. cost, target vendor note, drawing link. The system reserves the real material number (§2.3) and creates the material in Draft plus the MCR in Data Completion. • The BOM line references the real number from minute one; there is no renumbering event later, so CAD drawings annotated with the number never go stale. • Est. cost from the placeholder becomes the costing simulation basis until the Accounting task releases a real basis (info record award or first standard). Estimates consuming placeholder costs flag “contains placeholder pricing” on the quote margin panel (MFG Part B §3.2). • Placeholder aging report (MFG Part C §7) reads MCR ages; the 14-day exception remains.

4.4	Change	Governance	and	Duplicate	Prevention Control Specification Field change classes Class 1 — free edit by owner role (texts, notes, non-controlling attributes). Class 2 — governed edit: owner role + approval by the named second role, recorded with reason (Sellable override, valuation class, method, batch/serial flags pre-stock, MRP type, procurement type, PC). Class 3 — never editable (type, number, base UoM after posting); change = supersession. The class of every field is part of the field dictionary (Section 18) — the UI derives editability from it, not from per-screen code Material Change Request (MCH) Class 2 single changes and all mass changes (§16.1) run as MCH documents: proposed rows (material, field, old, new), approval per class rules, scheduled apply (immediate or effective-dated), full audit. MCH links to ECO when the change originates from engineering change control (MFG Part C §4.2 task “update material”) Duplicate check On create and on description edit: fuzzy match (trigram similarity ≥ 0.9) against descriptions + manufacturer part numbers within the same material group L1. Matches show side-by-side compare; creating anyway requires a “not a duplicate because…” note stored on the MCR. Hard block only on identical MPN + manufacturer (E-MM-02) Reference-data protection Deactivation/blocks run a where-used sweep (open POs, WOs, SOs, BOM lines, price rows, info records, source lists, inspection plans) and show the impact list before confirmation; the same sweep gates Obsolete (must be empty) per V-MM-20 4.5	Function-Specific	Blocks Blocks are additive chips on an Active/Restricted material, each with reason code, set-by and date; each blocks a specific document family rather than the whole record: Block Blocks Typical Reason Set By Purchasing Block New PRs/RFQs/POs (open POs receivable) Quality escalation on the only vendor; commercial dispute Purchasing Manager Sales Block New quotes/SO lines (open SOs shippable unless Shipping Block too) Recall candidate; margin review Sales Director 
Production Block New WOs; existing WOs continue unless Immediate-effectivity ECO says otherwise Design problem under ECR Engineering Manager Shipping Block PGI for this material Quality hold at product level (batch/serial holds stay in INV 6.2) Quality Manager 
Posting Block All stock movements (count correction transactions exempt) Valuation investigation; migration freeze Controller 
4.6	Supersession	(Follow-Up	Material) Screen ID MM-SUP-001 URL Pattern /materials/supersessions Purpose Controlled replacement of one material by another with stock run-out and document cutover 

=== PAGE 152 ===
ENTERPRISE ERP — UNIFIED BUILD SPECIFICATION  |  V1.0 CONFIDENTIAL 
CONFIDENTIAL Page 152 of 312 
• Supersession record SUP-#: predecessor, successor, relationship (One-for-One / One-for-Many note), run-out policy (Use-Up stock then switch / Immediate switch + stock disposition), effective date, initiating ECO/MCH link. • MRP behavior: with Use-Up, demand pegs to predecessor stock until exhausted, then proposals flip to the successor automatically (mirrors ECO use-up logic, MFG Part C §4.2). With Immediate, remaining predecessor stock routes to the disposition chosen (rework/scrap/RTV) via the ECO disposition engine. • Document behavior: open POs on the predecessor raise buyer tasks (change or receive-and-close); BOM lines flip per ECO effectivity; sales cross-references (customer part numbers) carry to the successor with a supersession note printed on order confirmations. • The predecessor moves Active → Restricted at record approval and → Obsolete when V-MM-20 conditions are met; the successor’s record links back for history. Traceability (MFG Part F) is unaffected: genealogy always stores the material that was actually consumed.

PART	B VALUATION	AND	AUTOMATIC	ACCOUNT	DETERMINATION This Part specifies the engine that turns every stock-relevant transaction in all four volumes into GL postings without any screen naming account numbers. It formalizes and supersedes the per-screen posting notes: those tables remain correct, but they are now outputs of this matrix, not independent rules. 
5.	Valuation	and	Account	Determination 5.1	Valuation	Classes The valuation class is the single attribute that connects a material to its balance-sheet account and its offset accounts. Defaults derive from material type + group and are confirmed in the MCR Accounting task. Valuation Class Default For Inventory Account (event key STOCK) VC-3000 Raw Materials RAW (all groups except OIL if Controller elects split) 1310 Raw Materials VC-3010 Raw Materials — Fluids RAW group OIL (optional split, Controller decision at config) 1310 (or split account if remapped) VC-7900 Semi-Finished SEMI 1310 per current policy (INV valuation) with WIP flow via 1320 during orders VC-7920 Finished Goods FIN 1330 Finished Goods VC-3100 Trading Goods TRADE 1330 (or 1310 per Controller mapping) VC-3060 Packaging PACK 1360 Packaging Materials on Hand VC-3040 Spares SPARE 1340 Spare Parts (or 1310 if not split) VC-3080 Consumables CONS (when inventory-managed) 1310 or direct expense per config 
5.2	Event	Keys	and	the	Determination	Matrix Every posting the Posting Service creates resolves accounts by (event key, valuation class) and, where noted, transaction qualifiers. Event keys are closed-list system constants; new business behavior means a new row in the matrix, never code that names an account. Event Key Fires On Resolves To (default mapping) STOCK Any valuated stock increase/decrease at inventory account level 13xx per valuation class (§5.1)

PART	C CROSS-MODULE	LINKAGE	CONTRACTS This Part is the answer to the question the review raised about material management linkages: for each neighboring module, exactly which material data it reads, which events flow in each direction, which validations bind the two sides, and which failure behaviors apply. Each contract is written so a developer can implement either side against it without reading the other module’s code. Events named here join the integration matrix in Section 19.

Restricted/Blocked/Obsolete are excluded from component search (E-ENG-08) ENG → MM Placeholder creation (description, group, UoM, est. cost, target vendor) Creates Draft material + MCR per §4.3; number reserved immediately ENG → MM Drawing links per material Document links (MFG Part C §5.1 Linked Objects) render on the material Documents tab; fabricated/made parts require a released drawing before MCR activation of a SEMI (task-level check in the Quality/Production tasks) ENG → MM ECO effects ECO tasks may raise MCH rows (governed field changes, e.g. new revision-relevant characteristics), supersessions (§4.6) and status changes (Production Block during Immediate effectivity). Build Note: The AVL (MFG Part G §2.2) keeps: rank, qualification status, FAI, quality rating. The info record keeps: commercial terms. The source list keeps: period governance. These three tables are joined by (material, vendor); do not duplicate fields across them — the MFG Part G AVL columns “Price Basis / Lead Time” become read-through views onto the info record after this volume. 8.	Linkage:	Material	↔	Production	and	MRP Direction Data Rule MM → PP Planning view fields (§3.6) MRP engine inputs; the workbench never reads planning data from anywhere else MM → PP Production view fields (§3.8) WO creation defaults: issue warehouse, tolerances, serialization/batch timing MM → PP Batch/serial profile (§15) Determines scan behavior at SP-08/SP-12 and genealogy granularity MM → PP Lifecycle status + Production Block WO create/release validations (E-PP-01 family extends with E-PP-08 “material blocked for production”) PP → MM Consumption actuals ADU (trailing 90-day) recompute for reorder arithmetic; ABC/XYZ classification job inputs PP → MM WO receipt/settlement events MAV updates (buy items consumed), standard-cost variance data to CVA; no direct field writes on the material Availability semantics (single definition, used by ATP, MRP, reservations and release checks): Available = On-hand Unrestricted − Reserved − QI Hold − Blocked − Customer Property; On-Order = open PO + open WO + in-transit receipts, dated. Special stock buckets (§14.1) are excluded from anonymous availability except SO-pegged stock, which is available only to its pegged SO line.

PART	D SPECIAL	STOCK	AND	OWNERSHIP	TYPES Standard stock is PE-owned, anonymous and warehouse-located. Real operations add stock that is somewhere else, owned by someone else, or dedicated to something. This Part gives each special stock type a defined bucket, availability rule and posting treatment. Buckets are visible on the material Stock Overview (§3.13) and the Real-Time Stock Ledger (INV 15.12). 
14.	Special	Stock	Types 14.1	Bucket	Overview Bucket Owner Valuated by PE In Anonymous ATP Established By Unrestricted / QI Hold / Blocked (standard) PE Yes Unrestricted only INV as built At Subcontractor (1315) PE Yes No (visible to the owning WO/PO) MFG Part D §10 / G §6 Vendor Consignment Vendor until withdrawal No (memo qty; §14.2) Configurable: counts as supply with flag “consignment” in MRP 
This volume 
Customer Property Customer No (non-valuated qty) No MFG Part A §3.5 (RMA repair path); generalized here for customer-supplied material in ETO jobs SO-Pegged Stock PE Yes Only for its SO line WO receipt of pegged orders (MFG Part B §4.3); formalized here In-Transit (1350) PE Yes No until receipt Transfers (INV 5.3) 14.2	Vendor	Consignment Consignment lets a vendor stock material in PE’s warehouse that remains vendor property until PE withdraws it. Target use: high-volume hardware and selected insulation from partner vendors. Activation is per (material, vendor): Special Procurement = Consignment on the Planning view plus a consignment-flagged info record. • Fill-up: consignment PO type (no commitment value) → GRN books quantity into the Consignment bucket for that vendor. No value posting; the statistical memo (1311) carries quantity × IR basis for insurance and disclosure reporting only. • Withdrawal: any consuming movement (issue to WO, transfer to own stock, sale) automatically converts ownership at the moment of consumption. The withdrawal posts the value entry below and writes a consignment settlement line for the vendor/period. • Settlement: monthly run per vendor aggregates withdrawals into a self-billing settlement document to AP (ACC 6 invoice created from settlement, no vendor invoice matching); price = IR basis valid at each withdrawal date; deviations at true-up post to 5215. 

=== PAGE 163 ===
ENTERPRISE ERP — UNIFIED BUILD SPECIFICATION  |  V1.0 CONFIDENTIAL 
CONFIDENTIAL Page 163 of 312 
• Counting: consignment stock counts like own stock (it is in PE custody); variances adjust the memo quantity and raise vendor reconciliation lines, never 5310. • Returns to vendor from consignment reverse quantity only. MRP treats consignment on-hand as supply with source tag so planners see the ownership difference. Accounting Entry — Consignment withdrawal (to production order) Debit Credit Amount Basis Notes 1320 WIP (order) 2120 Consignment Payable Clearing Withdrawn qty × IR basis at withdrawal date Event keys USAGE + CONSIG; CC from WC, PC parent item Accounting Entry — Consignment settlement (monthly, per vendor) Debit Credit Amount Basis Notes 2120 Consignment Payable Clearing 2100 AP (vendor) Sum of period withdrawals at IR basis Self-billing document; tax per vendor profile 5215 Consignment Price Variance 2120 (or reverse) True-up delta when a retroactive price correction applies Requires Controller approval; rare by design 
14.3	Customer	Property	(generalized) • Beyond RMA repair units (MFG Part A §3.5), ETO contracts sometimes include customer-supplied components (e.g. customer-specified relays). These receive as Customer Property: quantity tracked with full batch/serial and genealogy, zero value, never in ATP, never counted in E&O. • Consumption into a WO posts no material value; genealogy records the consumption normally so the as-built record is complete. The cost estimate flags customer-supplied lines at zero material cost with a note on the quote (MFG Part I §3.1). • Return of unused customer property ships on a zero-value delivery with a property return note; the SO closure checklist includes “customer property reconciled” when any was received. 14.4	SO-Pegged	Stock • FG receipt from a pegged WO lands in the SO-Pegged bucket keyed to the SO line (already the behavior in MFG Part B §4.3; the bucket makes it explicit and auditable). Pegged stock is invisible to anonymous ATP and cannot be picked for another order without a formal De-Peg action (Sales Director approval + reason; logged on both SO lines). • Valuation is standard, same as FG; the bucket is a stock dimension, not a valuation change.

PART	F MASS	MAINTENANCE,	MIGRATION	AND	DATA	QUALITY 16.	Stewardship	Toolset 16.1	Mass	Maintenance Screen ID MM-MCH-001 URL Pattern /materials/change-requests Purpose Governed mass changes across materials (the MCH object from §4.4) • Selection: filter set (type, group, status, buyer, planner, free field predicates) or uploaded material list; preview grid shows current values of the target fields. • Change definition: target field(s) + new value or expression (set, adjust %, find/replace within text); Class rules per §4.4 decide the approval chain; Class 3 fields are not selectable. • Simulation: full before/after grid, validation results per row (rows failing validations are excluded and listed), effect summary (e.g. “124 materials, 3 will lose active source”), export. • Apply: immediate or effective-dated; transactional per row with error isolation; result log downloadable; every row writes the standard field-level audit with the MCH reference. 16.2	Import	Templates XLSX templates per view (columns = fields with data dictionary validation on upload) support both migration and ongoing bulk creation. Uploads create MCRs/MCHs rather than writing directly, so governance is identical regardless of entry channel. Template versioning follows the field dictionary; an outdated template upload is rejected with a per-column diff. 16.3	Migration	Specification	(answers	review	D-04	for	MM	scope) Step Specification Source inventory Legacy item list extract with: code, description, type mapping candidate, UoM, on-hand by location, last costs, vendor/price data, open PO/SO/WO references. 17.3	Excess	&	Obsolete	Reserve	Run Screen ID MM-EOR-001 URL Pattern /materials/eo-reserve Purpose Quarterly (config) computation and posting of the E&O inventory reserve — Controller-owned • Policy table: aging buckets (no consumption 6/12/24+ months) × reserve % per material type; overrides per material with reason (e.g. insurance spares exempt). • Run: compute per material (on-hand value × bucket %), compare to existing reserve, propose delta; review grid with drill to MM-R4 evidence; Controller posts. • Posting: DR 6320 E&O Expense / CR 5340 Inventory Reserve for builds; reverse for releases (consumption resumed or stock scrapped — scrap first relieves the reserve, then 5320 takes only the unreserved remainder). • The reserve is a valuation adjustment account, never a quantity change; traceability and availability are unaffected.

18.2	Physical	Rules	(applies	to	every	MM	entity) • Surrogate integer/UUID PKs; business keys unique-indexed; foreign keys enforced in the database, not only the app. • Effective-dated tables never update rows in place: new row + closed predecessor, both retained (valuation, IR prices, account determination, source list). • Field-level audit via the shared audit stream (screen, MCH, migration, API as sources); audit rows are append-only and excluded from purge. • Concurrency: optimistic locking (version column) on the material and children; the MCR/MCH apply steps take row locks per material to prevent cross-workflow races. • Archive/purge: Obsolete materials archive after the retention period (default 10 years, aligned to genealogy retention) into the read-only store; material numbers are never reused. 18.3	API	and	Event	Surface Interface Specification REST: /api/v1/materials GET (filter set of §3.1), GET by id (view-shaped payloads), POST creates MCR (never a direct Active material), PATCH creates MCH rows for governed fields / direct write for Class 1 with audit; all responses carry the field dictionary version REST: /api/v1/info-records, /source-lists, /supersessions CRUD with the same governance semantics as the screens; idempotency keys on POST Events (outbox pattern) MaterialCreated, MaterialActivated, MaterialChanged (field list), MaterialStatusChanged, MaterialSuperseded, InfoRecordPriceChanged, SourceListChanged, StdCostReleased — versioned payloads, ordered per material, consumed by MRP cache, pricing, search index and any future PLM/CAD connector (review F-03 lands on this surface)

1.	Purpose	and	Coverage	Map This Book supplies the enterprise architecture layer of the specification. Functional depth alone does not constitute an enterprise build package. The flows must be proven closed across modules, and the platform, security, operational, planning, global and delivery requirements must exist as controlled content. This Book carries them. It is deliberately cross-cutting: it specifies behavior that spans the Inventory and Accounting specifications, Book I and Book II rather than repeating any of them. Ref Requirement Specified In P0-01 Version and change control, closure map Version and change control governed through the build package manifest; this table is the requirement to section map P0-02 Cross-volume flow closure matrix Part A: eight flows, each specified against the fifteen field closure template P0-03 Integration and transaction architecture Part B P0-04 Security architecture Part C P0-05 Resilience and operations Part D P0-06 Finite scheduling and CTP requirements Part E, including the explicit go-live boundary statement P0-07 Multi-plant, intercompany and global model Part F P0-08 CAD/PDM/PLM integration specification Part G P0-09 Test, migration, cutover and acceptance plan Part H Scope statement. This package carries the Phase 1 build scope with an architected path to the target state. Capabilities phased beyond first go live are listed in each Part with their trigger conditions and are specified at module depth in Book IX. External descriptions must use the benchmark language on the cover and nothing stronger. Build Note: Executed test evidence and executed sign off cannot be supplied by any document. Part H defines the traceability structure, the test classes and the acceptance gates so that evidence accumulates during the build. The sign off pages must remain blank until the named approvers execute them. 1.1	Reading	Conventions

PART	A CROSS-VOLUME	FLOW	CLOSURE	BOOK Each flow below is specified against the full fifteen field closure template. Every field cites the controlling specification section so the closure is verifiable, and every flow carries a reserved test-case range that Part H binds into the traceability matrix. A flow is accepted as closed only when its test range shows passing evidence for every row class: happy path, partial, cancellation, reversal, late posting, concurrency, integration failure, security and reconciliation. FL-01	—	Make-to-Stock	Order-to-Cash Closure Field Specification 1. Flow and objective Sell stocked components and standard transformers from FG with credit-safe, tax-correct, reconciled cash closure 2. Trigger and prerequisites Confirmed SO (MFG Part B §4.6) for MTS lines; customer Active with Bill-To/Ship-To; price resolved (MFG Part A §4.4); credit check passed (MFG Part B §2.2) 3. Source document and owner Sales Order; owner: Sales Admin until Confirmed, then fulfillment owned by Warehouse (pick/pack/ship) and Finance (invoice to cash) 4. PART	B INTEGRATION	AND	TRANSACTION	ARCHITECTURE This Part turns the integration matrices of the functional Books into executable contracts. It defines the platform standards every interface must obey, the transaction and consistency model, the interface catalog with ownership and reconciliation, and the operational tooling. 
2.	Platform	Standards 2.1	API	Standard Standard Specification Style and versioning REST over HTTPS, JSON payloads; URI-versioned (/api/v1/...); breaking changes require a new major version; N and N−1 supported concurrently; deprecation announced ≥ 90 days with response-header warnings and consumer-usage reporting before shutdown Schemas Every endpoint and event carries a published JSON Schema in the contract registry (source-controlled with the code); payloads validate at the boundary; unknown fields ignored on read, never emitted undocumented Authentication OAuth2 client-credentials for system callers; user-context calls carry the OIDC access token (Part C §2); every token scoped to named interfaces; no shared secrets in code (Part C §7) Authorization The API layer enforces the same role/entity/plant authorization as screens (single policy engine, Part C §4); no “trusted internal caller” bypass Idempotency All mutating endpoints require an Idempotency-Key header (UUID); the platform stores (key, caller, request hash, response) for 7 days and replays the stored response on retry; mismatched hash with same key returns 409 Errors RFC 7807 problem+json: type, title, status, detail, correlation ID, and the domain error code (the E-codes from the functional volumes travel here); 4xx never retried by clients, 5xx retried per policy below Pagination, filtering Cursor-based pagination; filter grammar consistent with listing screens; hard response cap with explicit continuation 2.2	Event	Standard Standard Specification Transport and format Durable log-based broker (Kafka-class) with JSON envelopes: event_id (UUID), event_type + schema version, occurred_at, producer, correlation_id, causation_id, entity context, payload 

=== PAGE 185 ===
ENTERPRISE ERP — UNIFIED BUILD SPECIFICATION  |  V1.0 CONFIDENTIAL 
CONFIDENTIAL Page 185 of 312 
Standard Specification Ordering Partition key = business object key (material_id, wo_id, so_id…) guaranteeing per-object ordering; consumers must not assume cross-object order Delivery and idempotency At-least-once delivery; every consumer keeps a processed-event store keyed on event_id (or a natural idempotency key named in the contract) — duplicate deliveries are no-ops by construction Retry and dead-letter Consumer retry with exponential backoff (1 s → 5 min cap) for 24 h, then dead-letter queue; DLQ items alert the owning team and are replayable from the integration console (§4) after cause resolution; DLQ replay preserves original event_id so idempotency still holds Replay Topics retain 30 days; full-history rebuild for read models via snapshot + replay procedure documented per consumer Schema evolution Additive changes only within a version; consumers tolerate unknown fields; breaking change = new event_type version, dual-published during migration window 2.3	Transaction	and	Consistency	Model • Atomic business transactions: the boundaries named in Part A field 9 (GRN, PGI, confirmation, IC pairing, disposition outcomes) execute as single database transactions covering stock, tracking, journal and outbox rows together. No business event is ever half-posted. • Transactional outbox: events and cross-module commands are written in the same transaction as the state change and published by a relay with at-least-once semantics. Direct dual-writes (DB + broker in separate steps) are prohibited. • Concurrency: optimistic locking (version column) on documents; pessimistic row locks only inside posting transactions (reservation firm-up, stock decrement, number-series allocation). Stock rows use atomic conditional decrements — negative-stock races are structurally impossible where policy forbids negatives. • Compensating actions: where a saga spans modules (RMA disposition outcomes, IC pairing rollback on partial config error), each step has a named compensator specified in the interface contract; sagas surface in the console with step-level state. • Clock and sequence: all timestamps UTC server-side; document numbers from gap-free series where finance requires (invoices) and gap-tolerant elsewhere (performance), per series configuration. 
3.	Interface	Catalog Every integration in the four volumes maps to a cataloged contract. Owner = the team accountable for the contract, its monitoring and its reconciliation. Contracts live in the registry with schemas; this table is the governance index. Integration	Operations	Console Element Specification Message monitor Per interface: throughput, lag, error rate, DLQ depth, oldest unprocessed age; drill to individual message with full envelope and correlation chain DLQ management Inspect, edit-and-replay (edit rights restricted + audited), bulk replay after fix, discard with mandatory reason (discard of a financially relevant command requires Controller) Correlation tracing correlation_id propagates from originating user action through every API call, event, job and journal line; a single search reconstructs the distributed flow (feeds the ≤ 3 clicks auditability NFR) Reconciliation reports The per-interface controls in §3 run scheduled; failures raise incidents per Part D §5, not emails that can be missed Contract tests Every contract has producer and consumer tests in CI (schema + behavior incl. idempotent-replay test); a contract change that breaks a consumer test cannot merge

PART	C SECURITY	AND	IDENTITY	ARCHITECTURE RBAC role tables exist throughout the functional Books. This Part supplies the enterprise identity, authorization, data protection and security-operations architecture those tables plug into. 
2.	Identity	and	Authentication Control Specification SSO OIDC (primary) with SAML 2.0 fallback against the corporate IdP (Entra ID assumed; confirm at kickoff — the design is IdP-agnostic). No local passwords for workforce accounts; local break-glass accounts only per §5 MFA Enforced at the IdP for all interactive access; step-up authentication (re-prompt) for: payment release, vendor bank changes, period reopen, matrix release (MM §5.3), user-admin actions Provisioning SCIM 2.0 from the IdP: joiner/mover/leaver automation; role assignment via IdP groups mapped to ERP roles; leaver deactivation SLA 1 hour from HR trigger; orphan-account report weekly Shop-floor sessions Kiosk terminals: device-bound certificate + operator badge scan opens a scoped session (WC-limited, per MFG D §6); inactivity lock 10 min (config per station class); badge-out on operation complete optional per plant policy; no shared browser sessions Service accounts OAuth2 client-credentials per integration (one client per IF-nn); secrets in the vault (§7), rotated ≤ 90 days, usage-monitored; no user-account API usage by systems External parties Vendors/customers (portal phases) authenticate through a separate external IdP tenant; internal and external identities never share a namespace 
3.	Authorization	Model • One policy engine, three layers: Role (what functions), Scope (which entity/plant/warehouse/cost-center rows), Field group (which attributes, per MM §3.2 ownership and ACC field rules). Screens, APIs and events all consult the same engine; there is no UI-only hiding of data (the blind-count rule INV/MFG A §7.3 is the model everywhere). • Roles are the ones named per module in the four volumes, catalogued centrally with their permission sets; new roles or permission changes ride change control with SoD analysis. • Separation of duties: the SoD rule set consolidates every pair named in the volumes (PO create vs GRN post vs invoice approve vs payment release; WO confirm vs settlement; price edit vs approve; count vs approve; matrix edit vs release; MCR task vs activation). The engine blocks toxic combinations at role-assignment time, not just at transaction time; exceptions require CFO approval with expiry date. • Row-level scope: every query filters by the user’s entity/plant grants server-side; documents render cross-entity only for explicitly granted group roles (Controller group view). • Restricted documents: export-controlled drawings (MFG C §5.1 Security Class) require the EAR user flag; access logged individually. • Recertification: quarterly access review per role owner with attestation; failed attestations auto-revoke; report retained as audit evidence. 
4.	Privileged	Access	and	Break-Glass 

=== PAGE 189 ===
ENTERPRISE ERP — UNIFIED BUILD SPECIFICATION  |  V1.0 CONFIDENTIAL 
CONFIDENTIAL Page 189 of 312 
Control Specification PAM Infrastructure and database access only through the PAM broker: just-in-time elevation, ticket-linked, session-recorded; standing admin access prohibited in production Production data access Developers have no default production access; diagnostic access via time-boxed grants with masked PII views unless the ticket justifies raw access (Controller/ISO approval for finance data) Break-glass Two sealed local emergency accounts (app + infra), credentials in escrow, use alarms the security channel immediately and forces post-incident review + rotation Change execution Deployments run from CI/CD service identities (Part D §4); humans do not apply schema or config changes by hand in production 
5.	Data	Protection Control Specification Encryption in transit TLS 1.2+ everywhere including internal service traffic and printer/scanner websocket wedge; no plaintext listeners Encryption at rest Storage-level encryption for databases, object storage and backups; column-level encryption for bank details and personal data fields (key separation from storage keys) Key and secret management Central vault: application secrets, API clients, encryption keys; automatic rotation schedules; access audited; certificates auto-renewed with expiry alerting ≥ 30 days PII inventory Personal data catalog: employee operator data (badges, labor records), customer contacts, vendor contacts; retention and access rules per class; recall exports (FL-08) logged as PII disclosures Export control EAR-restricted document class enforced (Part C §3); denied-party screening on new customers/vendors (Phase 2 service via IF catalog extension) with interim manual checklist at onboarding Data residency Phase 1 US region deployment; residency constraints re-evaluated at international expansion (Part F trigger) 
6.	Security	Operations • Security event logging: authentication events, authorization denials, privilege use, SoD exception use, data exports, break-glass, admin changes — shipped to the SIEM with 13-month retention minimum. • Alerting: brute-force patterns, impossible travel (IdP), privilege anomalies, DLQ tampering, mass-export anomalies; alerts route to the incident process (Part D §5) with security severity mapping. • Vulnerability management: dependency scanning in CI, monthly platform patching windows, annual penetration test whose findings enter the risk register with owners and dates; criticals block go-live (Part H gate). • Incident response: documented runbook with roles, evidence preservation, notification decision tree (customers, insurers, counsel); tabletop exercise annually alongside the recall drill.

PART	D RESILIENCE,	AVAILABILITY	AND	OPERATIONS Targets are set per business service, not as one global number, because the shop floor stopping is a different event from a report being slow. 
2.	Service	Tiers,	RTO	and	RPO Tier Business Services Availability Target RTO RPO T1 — Production-critical Shop-floor terminal, scanning, label printing, GRN/issue/PGI posting, WO confirmations 99.5% during production hours (existing target, now formalized as T1) 
1 hour 0 (committed transactions); ≤ 4 h of offline scan queue replays after recovery per MFG F §2 T2 — Business-day critical Order entry, purchasing, engineering, quality screens, MRP on-demand, costing 99.0% business hours 4 hours 15 minutes 
T3 — Periodic Nightly MRP, costing runs, allocations, close jobs, reporting/analytics Job-window commitments (below) 24 hours 24 hours (rerunnable jobs) Batch windows: nightly MRP completes by 05:00 plant time; label/scan services never share a maintenance window with production shifts; close-critical jobs have priority lanes in the last 3 and first 3 business days of each month. 
3.	Disaster	Recovery	and	Degraded	Modes Element Specification Topology Primary region multi-zone: database synchronous replication across zones (zero-RPO zone failure); warm standby in a second region with continuous async replication for regional disaster (RPO ≤ 15 min, RTO ≤ 4 h for T1/T2) Backups Continuous WAL archiving + daily full snapshots; 35-day point-in-time recovery; quarterly restore test to an isolated environment with checksum and row-count verification — restore evidence filed in the operations log (Part H gate input) Failover Zone failover automatic; region failover is a decided action (runbook, two-person rule) with DNS cutover and consumer replay from outbox/topics; annual full region-failover exercise in the staging replica Degraded modes Defined per dependency: broker down → outbox accumulates, postings continue, consumers catch up (monitor depth); tax engine down → MFG A §11.1 failover; IdP down → existing sessions continue, break-glass for new critical access; printer down → station failover printer + queued reprints; WAN down at plant → T1 terminals run the 4-hour offline queue, everything else read-only Data integrity on recovery Recovery always replays from the committed log; the outbox pattern (Part B §2.3) guarantees no lost events for committed transactions and idempotent consumers absorb replays 
4.	Deployment	and	Change 

=== PAGE 191 ===
ENTERPRISE ERP — UNIFIED BUILD SPECIFICATION  |  V1.0 CONFIDENTIAL 
CONFIDENTIAL Page 191 of 312 
• Environments: DEV → TEST (integrated, contract tests) → UAT (business validation, migration rehearsal target) → PROD; configuration promoted as versioned artifacts, never hand-edited upward. • CI/CD: trunk-based with mandatory review; pipeline gates: unit, contract (Part B §4), migration dry-run, security scans; artifacts immutable and signed. • Zero-downtime releases: rolling deploys behind health checks; database migrations expand-and-contract (additive first, code tolerant of both schemas, contraction after soak); feature flags for functional cutover so deploy ≠ release. • Rollback: every release has a tested rollback path (previous artifact + contract-compatible schema); flags allow functional rollback without redeploy; rollback drills part of M7. • Release calendar: T1-affecting releases outside production shifts; close-freeze on finance-touching releases during Hard Close days. 
5.	Observability	and	Support	Operations Element Specification Telemetry Metrics, structured logs and distributed traces on every service; correlation_id (Part B §4) joins traces to business documents; scan telemetry retained 24 months per MFG F §4 SLOs and error budgets Per NFR: scan round-trip < 400 ms P95, PGI < 2 s, detail load < 1.5 s, MRP < 30 min, traceability tree < 10 s, dispatch refresh < 2 s. Each is an SLO with burn-rate alerting; sustained budget burn freezes feature releases for the offending service until recovered Alerting and incidents Severity matrix (S1: T1 service down or data-integrity suspicion → 15-min response 24×7 during production; S2: T2 degradation → 1 h business hours; S3/S4 queued); on-call rotation; incident timeline, postmortem without blame, action items tracked to closure Job monitoring Batch framework with run history, SLA timers, dependency chains, auto-retry policy and failure escalation; failed close-critical jobs page, never just log Support model Tier 1 (plant super-users, runbook-driven), Tier 2 (application support, console access per Part C), Tier 3 (the development partner engineering with SLA per support contract); month-end hypercare roster for the first three closes Runbooks Written per degraded mode and per S1 scenario before go-live (Part H readiness gate item); stored versioned beside the code 
6.	Capacity	and	Retention • Capacity model: sized for the NFR volumes (MFG Part K §4, MM §20.2) with 3× headroom on scan peaks (shift start bursts) and 5-year growth; load, stress, soak and failover tests against these profiles are Part H test classes, executed in M7 with results filed as evidence. • Data retention: transactional data online 7 years; genealogy and quality evidence 10 years minimum (recall support, FL-08); released document files WORM per MFG NFR; scan telemetry 24 months; archives queryable read-only; legal hold flag suspends any purge per object.

PART	E FINITE	SCHEDULING	AND	CAPABLE-TO-PROMISE A contractual ETO promise date computed on infinite capacity is not defensible. This Part defines the constraint model, the scheduling engine requirements, the CTP check, the governance around promises, and an explicit statement of what ships at go-live versus what follows. The data model already carries operation durations, calendars, crews and alternates (MFG D), so this is an engine and policy layer, not a redesign. 
2.	Constraint	Model Constraint Source Data Scheduling Behavior Work center capacity WC capacity model: shifts, hours, parallel stations, efficiency (MFG D §2) Finite buckets per WC per shift; operations consume capacity; overload impossible in the finite plan (only in the released past) Batch resources Oven/vacuum cycle size and hours (MFG D §2) Cycle-level scheduling: orders pack into cycles; a cycle is one capacity consumption regardless of fill Labor and crews Crew size per op/WC; workforce calendar (new: crew roster per WC per shift, maintained by Production Scheduler) Operation needs both machine slot and crew slot; crew shortfall delays exactly like machine shortfall Skills and certifications Operator qualification register (new object QAL-: operator, skill, level, expiry; linked to training records) Ops flagged with required skill schedule only into shifts with a qualified operator; expired certification removes the operator from eligibility automatically (and interlocks the terminal at SP-07 login for flagged ops) Tooling and fixtures Tool register (new object TOOL-: tool, quantity, WC compatibility, maintenance status) Ops declaring a tool requirement consume tool availability; tools in maintenance are unavailable Material availability MRP supply picture incl. confirmed PO dates An operation cannot schedule before its op-mapped components’ availability dates (full-kit by operation, consistent with staging SP-09) Maintenance windows WC status Maintenance (MFG D §2) + planned maintenance calendar (new) Blocked capacity; planned windows visible in what-if before commitment Subcontract capacity Vendor lead times + declared weekly capacity on the AVL row (new field) Subcontract ops schedule against vendor capacity declarations; breach raises buyer exception rather than silent slip 
3.	Scheduling	Engine	Requirements • Modes: backward from due date (default), forward from release, and bottleneck-centered (schedule the constraint WC first — Test Lab and Vapor Phase Drying are the expected bottlenecks — then subordinate upstream/downstream). • Alternates: alternate WC with efficiency factor (existing field) is engine-selectable under load, logged as a decision; alternate routings are planner-selected only (engineering-controlled, never silent). • Setup families: sequence-dependent setup reduction by grouping (e.g. winding wire gauge families); family definitions per WC maintained by Manufacturing Engineer. 

=== PAGE 193 ===
ENTERPRISE ERP — UNIFIED BUILD SPECIFICATION  |  V1.0 CONFIDENTIAL 
CONFIDENTIAL Page 193 of 312 
• Freeze horizon: inside N days (default 5, per WC) the schedule does not auto-move; changes inside the freeze require Production Scheduler action with reason. Consistent with the material-level planning fence (MM §3.6). • What-if: sandbox copies of the schedule for scenario testing (rush order insertion, WC outage); publishing a scenario is an explicit replace action with diff preview; sandboxes never post. • Rescheduling policy: engine proposes moves as exceptions (EX-02/03 extended to operations); auto-apply only outside the freeze horizon and only within promise-date guardrails (§5); everything else is planner-confirmed. • Determinism and audit: same inputs produce the same schedule (seeded heuristics); every published schedule stores its input snapshot ID so any promise can be traced to the plan that made it. • Performance: full plant reschedule < 5 min at NFR volumes; single-order CTP < 10 s (the quote-time budget). 
4.	Capable-to-Promise Element Specification CTP check For a quote line or SO line: explode the (template or released) BOM and routing, check material availability dates (supply picture + info-record lead times for unstocked buys) AND finite capacity insertion across constrained WCs, returning the earliest feasible completion + ship date with the binding constraint named (“Test Lab week 34” / “bushing lead time 16 wks”) Quote integration Firm ETO quotes must carry a CTP result (replaces the feasibility check reference in MFG B §3.1); Budgetary quotes may quote lead-time bands without CTP, watermarked accordingly Promise capture Accepting a CTP result reserves soft capacity (decaying reservation, config 14 days) so parallel quotes do not double-promise the same bottleneck slot; SO confirmation converts to firm capacity load Revalidation Promise dates re-validate automatically on: ECO affecting the order, WC outage inside its path, supply slip on a pegged PO, schedule publish that moves its operations. A promise at risk raises the at-risk workflow (§5), never a silent new date ATP vs CTP boundary MTS lines keep the existing ATP (stock + scheduled receipts, MFG B §4.3); CTP applies to MTO/ETO. Global multi-plant ATP activates with Part F plant 2 
5.	Promise-Date	Governance • A customer-visible promise changes only through the Sales Change Order path with reason, impact and (per customer class policy) customer notification; planners move internal schedule, not promises. • At-risk workflow: projected completion beyond promise − transit buffer creates an at-risk record routed to Production Scheduler + Sales owner with recovery options (overtime shift, alternate WC, partial ship, re-promise); decisions logged against the order. • Promise reliability is a first-class KPI: promised-vs-actual distribution by product family and by binding constraint, monthly; CTP model calibration (planned vs actual durations) reviewed quarterly and fed back into routing standards.

PART	G CAD,	PDM	AND	PLM	INTEGRATION	SPECIFICATION The controlled document repository (MFG Part C §5) remains the ERP system of record for released renditions. This Part specifies the integration that connects native CAD authoring to it. Toolchain decision required at kickoff: this specification is written against SolidWorks + SolidWorks PDM Professional as the working baseline because it matches PE Engineering’s current authoring environment; every requirement is stated tool-neutrally so a different selection (Inventor/Vault, Creo/Windchill) changes the connector implementation, not the contract.

PART	H TEST,	MIGRATION,	CUTOVER	AND	ACCEPTANCE	FRAMEWORK This Part defines the structures that produce evidence during the build. It does not claim the evidence exists today; it makes its accumulation mandatory and auditable, and it gates go-live on it. 
2.	Requirements	Traceability • Requirement ID scheme: REQ-{VOL}-{SECTION}-{SEQ} generated by parsing the four functional volumes plus this volume: every field table, validation code, posting rule, status transition, button, interface and NFR becomes one or more requirement rows. The parse is mechanical (the volumes are structured for it) and the resulting register is the master RTM backbone, maintained in the ALM tool, exported monthly to the project record. • Every RTM row links: requirement → design/build ticket → test case ID(s) → latest execution result → defects → acceptance status. Requirements to test traceability for every screen, rule, transition, posting and integration is satisfied structurally: an RTM row without a test case is a visible gap count on the readiness dashboard, target zero at M7 exit. • Test case ID ranges reserved per flow in Part A field 15, plus module ranges (TC-MM-, TC-VAL-, TC-CAD-, TC-SEC-, TC-OPS-) — IDs are stable so evidence remains traceable across runs.

4.	Evidence	Standard • A test execution is evidence only with: test case ID + version, environment + build ID, data set reference, executor, timestamp, result, and artifacts (screens, journal extracts, report outputs) attached in the ALM record. • UAT sign-off is per business scenario pack (one per Part A flow) signed by the named business owner — these signatures are the content of the acceptance gate, collected as they pass, not at the end. • Defect policy at gates: zero open S1/S2 on gated scope; S3 with approved workarounds documented; the defect-to-requirement link keeps the RTM honest.

Book IV is the design governance layer of this specification. It records the mandatory scope decisions, resolves cross Book precedence, carries the three consolidated flows (Sales Order to Delivery, Design to Source, and the physical Traceability Contract), supplies the executable pricing condition model, the production version and planning algorithm baseline, the remaining module completion requirements, the architecture decision records, and the design and development gates with authoring standards. Where an artifact must live outside a specification (Figma prototypes, OpenAPI files, executed test evidence), this Book defines the artifact, its owner, its repository and its acceptance criterion, so the specification governs it without pretending to contain it.

Book IV has precedence over Books I–III where they conflict; within a Book, the conflict register §1.3 resolves precedence Book I — Manufacturing Core Contained Functional baseline for its modules Book II — Material Management Contained Controls the material object, valuation and account determination Book III — Enterprise Completeness Contained Controls flows FL-01…FL-08, integration, security, resilience, planning, global model, CAD integration and delivery framework Inventory & Warehouse Module Spec V3.0 (Mar 2026) — external Version-locked into the baseline. Deltas flagged “DELTA to INV x.x” in Books I–II control over the referenced INV section. Flow	A	—	Sales	Order	to	Delivery	(FL-09) FL-09 consolidates the fulfillment path that FL-01/FL-02 cover commercially into one execution design across MTS, MTO and ETO branches, and adds the objects the fulfillment design must carry: schedule lines, deterministic delivery split/merge, allocation and backorder processing, carrier/POD closure and controlled PGI reversal. Everything here extends Book I Part B and INV 5.7/Part A §5 without changing them. 2.1	Order	Schedule	Lines	(new	sub-object	of	the	SO	line) Field Type Req Specification Sched # Auto Auto 10, 20, 30 within the SO line; created automatically (one per line by default; more on split) Requested Qty / Date Number + date Yes Customer ask at schedule granularity; header/line dates default the first schedule Confirmed Qty / Date Number + date Auto Written by ATP (MTS) or CTP (MTO/ETO) with the supply/capacity constraint named and the plan snapshot ID stored (Book III Part E §3) Delivered Qty / Open Qty Numbers Auto Rolled from delivery postings; Open = Confirmed − Delivered − Cancelled Backorder Flag Badge Auto Set when Confirmed < Requested or a confirmed schedule slips; feeds the Backorder Workbench §2.2 Peg Reference Link Auto Reservation / WO / PR / planned order pegged to this schedule (pegging is schedule-level from this Book forward; line-level pegs in Book I read as schedule 10) Status Badge Auto Open → Confirmed → Allocated/Pegged → Delivery Due → Partially Delivered → Delivered → Closed / Cancelled Rules: delivery documents consume schedule lines, never raw order lines; a schedule line is the unit of confirmation, allocation, backorder and closure. Invoice-relevant quantities roll up from schedules to the line so ACC contracts are untouched. Migration: existing open SO lines convert to single-schedule lines automatically. 2.2	Allocation	and	Backorder	Processing • Allocation applies when available supply of an MTS material is less than the sum of confirmed schedules due in the horizon. Priority order: contractual customer buffers (Book I A §10.3) first,

then customer priority class (A/B/C on the customer master, Sales Director owned), then requested date, then order entry time. Fair-share within a class prorates by open quantity. • Backorder Workbench (screen OM-BO-001, /sales/backorders): all backordered and at-risk schedules with supply options (incoming POs/WOs with dates, substitutable stock per approved alternates), one-click re-promise proposals, and mass re-confirmation runs after each MRP run. different Incoterms; hazmat segregation (oil totes vs electronics per hazmat profile); over-dimensional units needing permits ship alone; customer “no partial” flag forces full-quantity waiting instead of split Split — planner elective Date-window separation (schedules > N days apart, default 7); equipment type differences (flatbed vs van); weight/volume over vehicle capacity auto-proposes a split with the load plan Merge — allowed Same customer + ship-to + Incoterms + date window across orders when the customer consolidation rule (Book I B §2.1 Commercial tab) permits; hazmat compatibility checked; merged deliveries keep per-order packing list sections Determinism The Delivery Creation run (nightly + on-demand from the cockpit) applies these rules identically every time; every split/merge decision stores the rule ID that fired, so an unexpected grouping is explainable (E-DLV-01 messages name the rule) 2.4	Delivery	Cockpit	and	Carrier/POD	Closure Element Specification Delivery Cockpit (OM-DLV-001, /sales/deliveries) Work queue of deliveries by status (Planned → Released → Picking → Packed → Quality Cleared → Loaded → PGI → Delivered/Closed) with readiness flags per Flow A stage 7 (supply, quality, credit, documents) on one object — the single answer to “can this ship today?” Load planning fields Vehicle/equipment type, permit checklist for over-dimensional loads (existing Book I B §5.1), loading sequence per HU, dock door and appointment slot (plant calendar); loading scan SP-18 confirms sequence Carrier and tracking (Phase 1 manual per DEC-04) Carrier, PRO/BOL #, planned transit days; tracking status manual until IF-21; expected-POD date = ship date + transit POD / Claim object (OM-POD) Per delivery: POD status Expected → Received / Exception → Resolved/Closed; receiver name, date, condition notes, signed document upload. Exceptions open a Claim record: type (shortage, damage, refusal), quantity/value, carrier claim reference, resolution (credit via CMR, redelivery, repair) — claims reuse RMA/credit paths, never invent parallel ones. Missing POD after expected date + N days alerts (W.2 rule) Final milestone POD receipt (or site acceptance for ETO) fires the Delivery/Commissioning milestone (Book I B §4.4) exactly once, idempotent on delivery ID 2.5	PGI	Reversal	and	Delivery	Correction	(controlled)

Case Rule Before invoice posted Shipping Manager may reverse PGI within the same fiscal period: atomic mirror of postings #25/#26/#27, serial statuses restored (Shipped → Reserved, customer/site cleared with history retained), HUs → Loaded, delivery → Packed, invoice request withdrawn. Reason mandatory; reversal references the original PGI document After invoice posted No direct PGI reversal. Path is credit/rebill (ACC 4) or RMA (FL-05) depending on whether goods physically returned; the delivery record links the correction chain Partial PGI failure Structurally impossible (single transaction, Book III Part B §2.3); a failed PGI leaves the delivery at Loaded with the error surfaced — never a half-posted state Genealogy Reversal writes reversal events per the Traceability Contract (§4.3); shipped events are never deleted Tests TC-O2C-061…070 added: reversal before/after invoice, same-period guard, serial restoration audit, double-reversal block, claim-driven credit 2.6	Flow	A	Design	Deliverables	and	Gate • BPMN swimlane set (Sales, Pricing, Credit/Finance, Planning, Engineering, Production, Warehouse, Quality, Shipping, Carrier, Accounting) and sequence diagrams for confirmation, promise, delivery creation, PGI, POD and billing — repository: the design vault, IDs BPMN-FLA-01…; acceptance: every diagram element carries the REQ/IF/TC references it implements. • UX pack for order workbench, promise dialog, allocation/backorder, delivery cockpit, pick/pack/load, PGI confirmation, POD/claim — Figma library per §9 standards, approved states per the screen template. • Test pack TC-FLA-001…080 covering the 16 stages incl. every mandatory exception class (duplicate customer PO, blocked customer, tax outage, soft-reservation expiry, split/merge determinism, wrong serial at pick, repack, failed PSI, carrier rejection, double PGI, POD exception, close reconciliation). 
3.	Flow	B	—	Design-to-Source:	Engineering	to	Purchasing	(FL-10) FL-10 is the dedicated design to source flow. It governs how an engineering need becomes a revision-correct, compliance-checked purchase, and how engineering change hits open purchase documents. It composes existing objects (ER, EBOM, MCR, AVL, IR, RFQ, PO, GRN, ECO) and adds three new controls: release levels, the long-lead purchase release, and the immutable supplier technical package. 3.1	Release	Levels	(new	control	on	the	design	package) Release Level Rule Concept Design exploration; nothing purchasable or buildable; placeholders may exist (MCR running) Preliminary / RFQ Package may go to suppliers for quotation only, watermarked NOT FOR MANUFACTURE; RFQs allowed, POs blocked (E-DTS-01) Long-Lead Purchase Release (LLR) Named long-lead items purchasable under §3.2 controls before full design release; production use still blocked Approved for Purchase Commercial/technical spec final for buying; POs unrestricted per normal rules; production release may still be pending

Release Level Rule Released for Production Existing meaning (Book I C §3.4/§6): MBOM/routing/drawings/plans effective for WOs Superseded / Obsolete Existing meanings; open-document and stock disposition per ECO/supersession engines The release level lives on the Engineering Request deliverables package and stamps every document and EBOM revision it covers; RFQ and PO line validations read it. Levels only move forward except via ECO. 3.2	Long-Lead	Purchase	Release	(LLR	object) Field / Rule Specification LLR-{YYYY}-{SEQ:4} Items (long-lead flagged: core steel, copper, bushings, tap changers per estimate), quantity and value caps, revision basis (exact package version §3.3), risk class (Low: catalog component / Medium: parametric spec stable / High: geometry still moving) Approvals Engineering Manager + Purchasing Manager; Controller joins above $100k exposure or risk class High; approval records the accepted cancellation exposure PO behavior POs created under an LLR carry the flag Long-Lead — Preliminary Revision and the package version; vendor terms must include the negotiated change/cancellation clause reference Exposure tracking Running exposure = open LLR PO value + cancellation fees; visible on the ETO order cost panel (feeds committed cost, §7.6) and on the LLR record; breaching the cap blocks further POs (E-DTS-02) Change handling Any ECO touching an LLR item forces the ECO open-document disposition (Book I C §4.2) plus a commercial decision per PO: proceed / change order to vendor / stop with exposure booking; unresolved LLR impacts block ECO release Closure LLR closes when final release confirms the purchased revision (no-change path) or every affected PO has a disposition (change path); the ETO order cannot invoice its Materials milestone with an open LLR exposure decision 3.3	Supplier	Technical	Package	(STP	object) Element Specification STP-{SEQ:6} Immutable bundle: drawing IDs + revisions (released renditions only), specifications, characteristics/tolerances extract, quality clauses (Q1–Q5), certificate requirements, origin/compliance requirements, acceptance criteria; SHA-256 checksum over the manifest Versioning Any content change = new STP version; old versions never mutate. RFQ and PO lines reference exactly one STP version; a superseded STP cannot attach to new documents (E-DTS-03) Vendor acknowledgement Confirmation capture (IF-02) extends with package acknowledgement: vendor confirms qty/date/price AND the STP version; state Acknowledged / Exception (deviation raised) / Rejected. Receipt against a PO whose vendor never acknowledged a revised STP hard-blocks (E-DTS-04) Deviation requests Vendor-proposed alternates/deviations create a Supplier Deviation record routed to Engineering + Quality (workflow W.4 addition); approval scope: affected lots/orders, expiry; approved deviations link into genealogy so affected serials are queryable (Traceability rule §4.3)

4.2	Mandatory	Event	Schema • Identity: event_id (UUID), event_type + schema_version, business object key, entity, plant, source transaction + line. • Causality: correlation_id, causation_id, prior/reversal event reference, user/device/integration principal, originating requirement (REQ id) where rule-driven. • Time: occurred_at UTC, recorded_at UTC, original offline timestamp when replayed (Book I F §2), monotonic sequence within the object. • Product: material, revision/effectivity actually used (not current — rule §4.3), batch/serial, parent/child, qty + UoM. • Location/ownership: warehouse/bin/status, owner type/ref, peg type/ref, supplier/customer/site.

ENTERPRISE ERP — UNIFIED BUILD SPECIFICATION  |  V1.0 CONFIDENTIAL 
CONFIDENTIAL Page 212 of 312 
• Process references: PO/GRN/SO/delivery/WO/op/inspection/HU/shipment/RMA/campaign as applicable per event type (dictionary defines the required set per type). • Evidence: certificate/document/test/instrument links with checksums, result/disposition. • Integrity: append-only insert, row hash chained per object (tamper evidence), correction status (Active / Reversed / Replacement), retention class. 4.3	Correction	and	Reconciliation	Rules	(binding) Rule Required Behavior No destructive edit Genealogy rows are never updated or deleted. Corrections = reversal event + replacement event, both referencing the original (correction UI guides the pair). Database privileges enforce this, not convention Transaction/event parity Every traceability-relevant posting produces exactly one accepted event; every event resolves to a valid source transaction or an approved manual-exception record. Daily parity job counts both sides per type Serial uniqueness A serial has at most one active location, HU, parent and ownership state; violations are structurally prevented by the active-link constraint (serial_link model, Book I F §5) and monitored Batch quantity balance Received + produced + positive adjustments = on-hand + consumed + scrapped + shipped + returned, within UoM rounding tolerance; imbalance rows go to the completeness dashboard with owner Revision proof Consumption and shipment events store the engineering/document revision actually effective at execution (from the WO frozen revisions), never resolved-at-query-time Certificate lineage Certificate files and checksums are immutable; a replacement certificate links the original with reason; STP references (§3.3) keep supplier package lineage Recall completeness Campaign population is versioned; closure requires 100% accounted (recovered / field-corrected / documented exception) per FL-08 field 12 Daily controls Automated: orphan events, missing events, broken parent-child links, invalid status sequences, source/event count mismatches — reported with assigned owners on the Traceability Completeness dashboard (coverage %, orphan counts, delayed offline events, unresolved corrections) 4.4	Required	Outputs	and	Tests • Outputs (extending INV 10.4): backward trace, forward trace, as-built record (incl. rework and removed components), recall population report, certificate dossier (customer/audit package: CoC, FTR, material certs, drawing/as-built references, shipment evidence — one-click per serial), completeness dashboard. Performance: full tree < 10 s (existing NFR); archive queries < 60 s (Book III D §6 retention tiers). • Test pack TC-TRC-001…040 must cover, at minimum: multi-WO forward trace from one heat, backward trace through rework with removed/replaced children, offline scan replay with conflict and no duplicates, wrong-batch correction with explainable history, mock recall end-to-end, archive-and-retrieve at performance target.

5.1	Condition	Types	and	Calculation	Schema Step Condition Type Source (access sequence) Behavior 10 PB00 Base Price 1) Manual override (authority-checked) 2) Customer-specific price 3) Price list rate with qty breaks 4) Item default (fixed/markup) First hit wins; Price Source badge stores which access supplied it; below-floor detection runs after step 40 20 DIS1 Discount % Document line entry within role limit; contract discount matrix (agreements §6.2 Book I I) Applies to PB00; stacked discounts prohibited (one DIS1 per line) 30 SURC Commodity Surcharge Surcharge rule per item/family (Book I I §6.4): index basis, content, pass-through Separate element, printed separately; re-fires only at defined repricing events 40 FRT1 Freight Per Incoterms policy: included (no element) / estimated pass-through (accrual basis) / separately billed line Never buried in PB00; margin calculations exclude pass-through freight 50 TAX External engine per Book I A §11 precedence Committed at invoice; estimates at quote/SO save 5.2	Binding	Calculation	Rules • Persisted price elements: every document line stores its condition results as rows (type, source row ID, basis, rate, value, currency) at determination time — documents never re-derive prices from masters afterwards. Repricing replaces the element set explicitly with a diff dialog (existing rule, now at element granularity). • Currency: conversion at document level only, using the daily rate of the pricing date; price masters never auto-convert; the booked rate is stored on the document (existing ACC 3 rule, restated as the condition-model rule). • Rounding: each condition value rounds half-up to currency minor unit at line level; the document total is the sum of rounded lines (matches the tax engine, Book I A §11.2); price unit arithmetic (per 100/1000) resolves before rounding. • UoM: condition rates apply per Sales UoM; conversion to base for margin uses the rational factors (Book II §2.5); a missing conversion path blocks determination (existing V-SAL rule). • Validity and overlap: all condition records are effective-dated, non-overlapping per key (V-SAL-02/03 generalized to every condition table); retroactive master changes never restate documents — corrections go through credit/rebill (the Book I A §11.5 doctrine applies to prices as well as tax). • Explainability: the simulation endpoint (formalizing the Price Simulation button) is /api/v1/pricing/simulate — input customer, item, qty, date; output the full element set with every access tried, the winning source rows and the floor check; same service the documents call, guaranteeing simulation = reality.

Field Type Req Specification MBOM Revision / Routing Revision Lookups Yes Both must be Released; pairing is the point — no more independent selection Inspection Plan Set Auto view — INP/FIN plans resolving for this pairing (preview per Book I A §6.2) Lot Size Range Numbers No From/to qty this version applies to (e.g. prototype vs series routing) Valid From / To Dates Yes/No Effective dating; exactly one valid PV per (material, plant, lot-size band, date) — V-PV-01 blocks overlaps Status Badge Auto Draft → Released (Engineering Manager) → Superseded/Obsolete; ECO cutover swaps PVs atomically Selection rule: WO creation and MRP explosion select the valid production version — never a bare MBOM or routing. Existing single-pair materials migrate to auto-generated PV-1 records, so Book I Part D behavior is unchanged where only one version exists. Plant-2 activation (Book III Part F §5) rides this object as designed. 6.2	MRP	Algorithm	Baseline	and	Golden	Data • The deterministic order of the run is binding: (1) low-level-code assignment; (2) per material by LLC ascending: gross requirements aggregation by bucket (daily), (3) netting: net(t) = max(0, gross(t) + safety stock − projected available(t)), projected available rolls supply at confirmed dates; (4) lot sizing per policy with rounding; (5) scrap application (component + assembly, Book I C §3.2 / Book II §3.6); (6) lead-time offsetting (routing-based or field-based per Book II §3.6, calendars respected); (7) source assignment per Book II §7.5; (8) exception generation EX-01…EX-09; (9) firmed proposals untouched; planning fences respected (EX-08). • Worked example: the specification repository carries the golden dataset GD-MRP-01: a 3-level BOM (transformer, coil sub-assembly, copper), seeded demand and supply, expected planned orders and exceptions per run — the regression anchor for every engine change. Property test: rerunning MRP on unchanged inputs yields byte-identical proposals (determinism NFR, mirrors Book III Part E §3). • Pegging: full multi-level pegging is stored per run (requirement → supply chain), powering the Backorder Workbench (§2.2), shortage explanations and promise revalidation. De-pegging follows §2.2 approval rules. • Plan snapshots: each published schedule/MRP run stores its input snapshot ID (existing Part E rule) so every confirmed date names the plan that produced it. 
7.	Module	Completion	Additions The remaining module completion requirements are closed here. Requirements that demand artifacts (diagrams, prototypes, schema files, test evidence) are governed by the §9 gates; requirements already specified in Books I–III are cross referenced in the module index and not repeated. 7.1	Material	Duplicate	Merge	(completes	Book	II	§4.4) • Matching: trigram similarity on normalized descriptions + exact MPN/manufacturer matches, threshold 0.9 review / 0.97 auto-flag, scoped within material group L1 (existing); the steward review queue shows side-by-side with usage counts.

• Merge = supersession specialization: survivor chosen by steward; loser becomes Restricted with supersession record (Book II §4.6); open references rewrite where safe (BOM lines via ECO task, price rows re-keyed with approval, info records merged keeping best basis with history), stock transfers to survivor batch-by-batch with genealogy continuity events; unresolvable references block the merge with a worklist. Merges are irreversible by design — the audit chain is the undo story. 7.2	Warehouse	Task	Model	and	Labor	Time	Boundary • Warehouse tasks (receiving, putaway, replenishment, pick, stage, pack, load, transfer) become explicit task records with assignment, device, status (Queued → Assigned → In Progress → Done/Exception) layered on the existing documents (picklists, putaway proposals) — the mobile work queue reads tasks, documents stay the system of record. No behavior change to INV postings. • Labor time boundary: shop-floor labor capture (Book I D §6) is for costing and genealogy only. It is not a payroll system: no pay rules, no attendance. The time-system boundary exports approved labor confirmations (operator, WO/op, hours) to the payroll/time system as IF-22 (reserved, Phase 2); supervisor corrections follow the confirmation-correction rule (mirror postings) with dual sign-off. 7.3	Quality	Deviation	/	Waiver	Control	(new	object,	go-live	per	DEC-09) Field / Rule Specification DEV-{YYYY}-{SEQ:4} Scope: material / batch / WO / serial set / customer; customer approval evidence mandatory when the customer flag or contract requires (attachment + reference); Controller when value impact > threshold Validity Expiry date or quantity cap, whichever first; auto-expiry job blocks further use and alerts (W.2/W.3) Traceability Affected serials/batches link to the deviation (genealogy evidence rows); the as-built record and certificate dossier disclose active deviations (FL-11 §4.4) Interaction A deviation never edits the inspection plan; Element Specification Entitlement check service /api/v1/warranty/entitlement (serial, claim date) → covered / not covered / conditional with rule trail; the RMA warranty toggle default (Book I A §3.4) now calls this service instead of the bare serial window — same outcome for the base case, correct outcome for extensions and transfers Migration Shipped serial history converts to IB records with ship-date warranty; unknown sites flagged for CS enrichment (data-quality queue) 7.5	Reporting	and	Analytics	Governance • KPI dictionary: one controlled row per KPI — formula, grain, filters, owner, target, source lineage, freshness class. Seed catalog: OTD %, promise reliability (Part E §5), FPY by gate, scrap value %, PPV/IPV, inventory turns, WIP aging, quote win rate, backlog (BBB), three-view order margin, DSO (ACC), close cycle days, traceability coverage %, data completeness (Book II §16.4), SLO attainment. A number that appears on two dashboards from two formulas is a defect. • Semantic model: facts (orders, deliveries, invoices, stock movements, confirmations, inspections, genealogy events, journal lines) and conformed dimensions (material, customer, vendor, plant/entity, work center, cost/profit center, calendar) defined once in the reporting layer (replica per ADR-05); operational reports that act as subledgers (WIP, GR/IR, AR/AP aging, stock ledger) stay in the transactional system and reconcile to GL per the close checklist — analytics never becomes the system of record. • Freshness classes: real-time (operational queues), 15-minute (dashboards), daily (analytics); late-arriving offline scans backfill with recorded_at lineage so daily numbers are restated visibly, not silently. • Security: the same role/entity/plant scopes apply to dashboards, exports and APIs (single policy engine, Book III Part C §3); exports of customer-identifying traceability data log as disclosures. • Acceptance: every shipped report carries a golden dataset with expected totals and drill paths (TC-RPT range). 7.6	ETO	Cost	Control	Additions	(completes	Book	I	Part	I	for	ETO) • Per ETO order cost panel: estimate (CE basis) vs committed (open POs + LLR exposure §3.2 + subcontract commitments) vs actual-to-date (WIP charges) vs estimate-at-completion (actual + remaining standard) vs margin-at-completion against the quoted price; change orders re-baseline with history. • EAC formula is deterministic: EAC = actuals + (remaining operations at standard rates) + (unissued material at current basis) + open commitments not yet consumed; variances against CE feed the quote-calibration review (Part E §5 promise calibration sibling). • This is order-level project cost control; full project/WBS accounting stays out of scope per DEC-06/DEC-10 boundaries until a Phase 3 decision. 7.7	Workflow	Engine	Schema	and	Runtime	(completes	Section	W) • Definition schema (stored, versioned): { workflow_id, version, document_type, trigger, conditions[] (field, operator, value — the rule grammar), steps[] (order, type sequential/parallel, approver resolution role/user/derived, sla_hours, escalation_target), terminal_actions[], effective_from/to, owner, approval_ref }. The W.4 catalog rows are instances of this schema; an admin UI edits definitions under change control.

• Runtime guarantees: idempotent action handling (double-click and email-link replay safe), duplicate-step suppression, timeout → escalation per step, recall/resubmit preserving history, compensation on terminal-action failure (the document returns to pre-approval state with the failure surfaced), full audit per action. • Notification templates: versioned templates per event with localization slots (English at go-live per Book III Part F §6), sensitive-data rules (no prices in shop-floor notifications; no customer PII in vendor-facing mails), signed action links expiring per policy, per-user digest preferences for non-critical severities. • Job contracts: every W.3 job declares inputs, dependency jobs, calendar, close-criticality, retry policy, idempotency key, output artifact and reconciliation query — rows in the job catalog, monitored per Book III Part D §5. • Workflow analytics: SLA attainment, aging by step, bottleneck approvers, rejection rates, delegation usage, override frequency — monthly review by each workflow owner (feeds the W.4 governance loop).

One deployable at go-live scale (NFR volumes are modest for modern hardware); context boundaries enforced by module visibility rules and the interface catalog so later extraction of a hot context (e.g. Traceability) is a deployment change, not a rewrite

Every IF catalog row publishes against this template in the contract repository.
