Book V is written for the people who will design and build the screens: UX designers, Figma authors, and front-end developers. It uses plain language, pictures and short tables. It removes nothing from Books I–IV; every topic here points to the exact section where the full field-level rules live. Read this Book first, design from it, and open the referenced sections only when a rule detail is needed. 
1.	How	to	Use	This	Book If you are… Read in this order Then design A UX designer starting cold §2 (what the system is, in SAP terms) → §3 (structure) → §5 (flows) → §7 (your module’s brief) → §11 (design system) The module’s “prototype first” list, using the screen inventory and the states checklist A designer doing the shop floor §3 → §5.4 → §7.6 (Production) → §8 (barcoding and devices) → §11 Kiosk screens: dispatch, start/complete, material scan, QC gate, hold A developer joining mid-build §2 glossary → §4 (master data) → §5 (flows) → §6 (linkages) → Book IV §8 (architecture) Against the field tables of Books I–III and the contracts of Books III–IV A reviewer verifying capability coverage §2 capability map Nothing to design — the map states what exists, where, and in which phase One rule keeps this simple: every screen in this system is specified twice. Once here, briefly, so you can find it and understand its job. Once in Books I–III, completely, with every field, validation and posting. The screen inventory tables in §7 carry both references.

Capability (benchmark term) What It Does (plain words) Specified In Phase Group Reporting / Consolidation Multi-entity consolidation, eliminations Feeds specified FL-07/Book III F §4; consolidation itself ACC scope / Phase 2 2.1	Plain-Language	Glossary	(the	36	terms	designers	will	meet) Term Meaning in one sentence Material Master The single record for anything the company buys, makes, stocks or sells; every module reads its own view of it Material Type The category (raw, semi-finished, finished, trading, packaging, spare, consumable, service) that decides how a material behaves everywhere BOM / EBOM / MBOM The parts list of a product; EBOM is how engineering designs it, MBOM is how the factory builds it Routing The ordered list of operations (with times and work centers) to build something Work Center A machine, station or crew where operations happen; carries capacity and hourly rates Production Version The approved pairing of one MBOM and one routing for a plant; orders always pick a version, never loose pieces MRP The nightly engine that compares demand with supply and proposes orders to make or buy ATP / CTP ATP answers “is there stock by that date?”; CTP answers “can we make it by that date?” checking materials AND capacity Planned Order MRP’s proposal to make something; a planner converts it into a real production order Production Order (WO) The document that authorizes building one item, collects its costs and carries its serial Backflush Components consumed automatically when an operation confirms, instead of scanning each issue Kitting / Staging Picking all components for an operation and delivering them to the work center before it starts Confirmation The operator’s report at an operation: good quantity, scrap, time; it posts labor and materials WIP Work in process: the money value sitting inside unfinished orders Settlement Closing an order’s books: comparing actual cost with standard and posting the differences (variances) Standard Cost The frozen planned cost of an item, rebuilt by a costing run; the yardstick everything is measured against Moving Average A cost that re-averages with every purchase receipt; used for bought items Valuation Class The tag on a material deciding which balance-sheet account its stock value sits in PPV Purchase price variance: what you paid minus what the standard said it should cost GR/IR A clearing account between goods received and invoice received; it must always net out Three-Way Match Invoice pays only when PO, goods receipt and invoice agree PGI Post Goods Issue: the moment shipped stock leaves the books and cost of goods sold posts 

=== PAGE 225 ===
ENTERPRISE ERP — UNIFIED BUILD SPECIFICATION  |  V1.0 CONFIDENTIAL 
CONFIDENTIAL Page 225 of 312 
Term Meaning in one sentence Condition Technique Pricing built from stacked elements (base price, discount, surcharge, freight, tax) resolved by precedence rules Info Record The commercial memory of one vendor supplying one material: price, lead time, order rules Source List / AVL Which vendors are allowed (AVL) and which one is fixed for a period (source list) Handling Unit (HU) A numbered physical package (crate, pallet, reel) with contents, weights and its own barcode SSCC The GS1 standard 18-digit license plate for a shipping unit; printed as GS1-128 barcode Batch / Heat A lot of material tracked as one identity; for steel and copper the mill heat number is the key Serial One physical unit tracked individually for life: build, test, customer, returns Genealogy The permanent family tree of a serial: every batch, child serial, operator, test and shipment it touched ECR / ECO Change request (asks and analyzes) and change order (executes) for engineering changes Effectivity When a change takes effect: by date, by serial number, or immediately FEFO First-expired, first-out picking for shelf-life materials Safety Stock A quantity floor MRP protects so demand spikes do not empty the shelf Milestone Billing Invoicing an ETO order in agreed slices (down payment, design approval, tested, shipped, delivered) ASC 606 The revenue accounting standard deciding when invoiced money counts as earned revenue

7.	Module	Design	Briefs One brief per module: what it is for, who uses it, every screen with its reference, and what to prototype first. Screen types: List (filterable register), Detail (tabbed record), Workbench (act-on-many queue), Wizard (guided steps), Kiosk (touch + scanner), Config (admin). 7.1	Sales	&	Order	Management Turns customer demand into confirmed, promised, shippable orders and carries them to invoiced closure. The heart is the sales order with schedule lines; everything else serves it. Who uses it: Customer service, sales reps, sales admin, shipping office, credit/finance. Screen Type What the user does here Spec Leads & Opportunities (OM-LEAD-001/2) List/Detail Capture demand before a customer exists; prior build lookup; convert to quotation Book VI §4.1 Customer List / Detail (OM-CUST-001/2) List/Detail Maintain customers: addresses, commercial terms, credit view, tax, history Book I B §2 Quotation List / Editor (OM-QT-001/2) List/Detail Build priced, versioned offers; ETO configuration panel; margin check Book I B §3 Sales Order List / Detail (OM-SO-001/2) List/Detail The contract: lines, schedule lines, pricing elements, milestones, pegs, changes Book I B §4; Book IV §2.1 Backorder Workbench (OM-BO-001) Workbench Fix shortages: reallocate, re-promise, split, substitute Book IV §2.2 Delivery Cockpit (OM-DLV-001) Workbench Group schedules into deliveries; watch readiness; release to warehouse Book IV §2.4 Shipment Detail (OM-SHP-002) Detail Carrier, permits, HUs, documents, PGI Book I B §5 Pack Station (OM-PACK-STATION-001) Kiosk Scan items into handling units; weights; labels Book I A §5 POD / Claims (OM-POD) List/Detail Record delivery proof; open and resolve claims Book IV §2.4 Key lifecycles: Quote: Draft → Issued → Won/Lost. Order: Draft → Confirmed → In Fulfillment → Shipped → Invoiced/Closed (credit hold branch). Schedule line: Open → Confirmed → Delivered. Delivery: Planned → …→ PGI → Closed.

 Figure V-16. Batch label, 4×2 in, as printed at receiving: batch barcode, heat number, vendor, origin, quantity, QI status at print. 8.4	Scan	Interaction	Rules	(design	law	for	every	scan	screen) • Focus lock: the scan field owns input; a scan is an event, never keystrokes into whatever field has focus (wedge service, ADR-10). Designers never place a scan target inside a form field. 


=== PAGE 241 ===
ENTERPRISE ERP — UNIFIED BUILD SPECIFICATION  |  V1.0 CONFIDENTIAL 
CONFIDENTIAL Page 241 of 312 
• Feedback in under 400 ms: success = single beep + green flash of the scanned row; failure = double low beep + red banner stating what was scanned, what was expected, the E-code, and the next action (Book I F §4). No silent failures, no toast that disappears before a gloved operator looks up. • One action per scan where possible: scanning a traveler op barcode starts the operation; scanning it again opens completion. Menus are for exceptions. • Big targets: minimum 48 px touch targets, 16 px base font on kiosks, high-contrast state colors from §11; test with gloves at arm’s length. • Offline visibly: when the terminal queues offline (up to 4 h, Book I F §2), a persistent amber banner shows queue depth; replay conflicts land in the supervisor exception queue, never silently dropped. • Reprint is governed: every reprint asks a reason; serial/nameplate reprints need Quality approval; duplicates watermark DUPLICATE (Book I F §4). 
9.	Traceability	at	a	Glance  Figure V-17. The genealogy chain T01–T13. Every link is an append-only event; corrections add reversal + replacement events, never edits (Book IV §4). For designers, traceability is two screens: the trace explorer (enter a serial or a heat number, see the tree in both directions with drill-down into every node’s evidence) and the completeness dashboard (coverage, orphans, unresolved corrections). Both are specified in Book IV §4.4; the tree must render in under 10 seconds and read like a family tree, not a log file.

Book VI is the build plan at screen level. Every screen named anywhere in this document appears below with one implementation entry. Each entry states the route, the layout to build, the actions, where the user arrives from, what the screen creates, which postings and scans it triggers, its build wave and the section that carries its complete field rules. The linkage question is answered inside every entry. The rows named Arrives from and Creates or updates are the linkage, screen by screen, with the journal template numbers and scan points beside them. Nothing earlier in the document is changed by this Book. 
1.	Method • Entries are grouped by module in build order. List and detail pairs share one entry when they ship together. • Build waves follow the roadmap in Book I Part K. Wave 1 runs weeks 1 to 4, wave 2 weeks 3 to 8, wave 3 weeks 6 to 12, wave 4 weeks 10 to 18, wave 5 weeks 14 to 20, wave 6 weeks 16 to 22, wave 7 weeks 20 to 26. • Every entry reuses the component inventory of Book V section 10.2. A layout cell names components, not pixels. The Figma frame set per screen follows the nine states of Book V section 10.3. • Posting references use the journal template numbers of the unified map. Scan references use the SP numbers of Book I Part F. • A developer finishing an entry has one definition of done. The screen passes gate DG-3 of Book IV section 9.3 with its field rules from the referenced section. 
2. 4.1	Lead	and	Opportunity	Object,	Field	Rules The lead is the first formal record of demand and precedes the customer master. It must let sales answer the repeat build question before engineering spends a single hour. The field rules below are the complete authority for this object. Field Group Fields and Rules Identity lead_id auto assigned from the LD number series. Source dropdown with the values Referral, Trade Show, Website, Direct, Agent, Other. Owner defaults to the creator and reassigns only by the Sales Director. Region from the shared region table Contact Company name mandatory, contact name, title, email, phone, address block. Duplicate check on save against company name and email domain across leads and customers, same dialog pattern as the material duplicate check Requirement The quotation configuration attribute set, every field optional at this stage: kVA rating, primary voltage, secondary voltage, phases, frequency, vector group, cooling class, impedance percent, tap arrangement, applicable standards, special requirements text. Values entered here flow to the quotation unchanged Prior build Read only panel populated by the prior build search. Stores the matched design reference, match score and the decision New Design, Variant of Existing or Repeat Build. The decision copies to the engineering request when the order later confirms Commercial Estimated value, estimated award date, competitor field, win probability percent defaulted by stage and manually adjustable, currency from the entity Stage Open, Qualified, Quoting, Won, Lost, Disqualified. Open to Qualified requires the qualification checklist complete. Qualified to Quoting happens automatically at quotation creation. Won and Lost set only by the win or loss recording on the linked quotation and never by hand. Disqualified requires a reason code • Conversion must create the customer with status Prospect, no credit limit and no financial views until Finance activates it through the normal customer master path. A prospect customer must not be selectable on a sales order. • The pipeline report must show count and value by stage, conversion rate stage to stage, win rate by source and by product class, and average age per stage. The existing Quote Funnel report gains the lead stages ahead of its first column and reads from this object. • Validation register. V-OM-40 duplicate warning on company and domain. V-OM-41 stage transitions only along the defined path. V-OM-42 conversion blocked while mandatory contact fields are empty. E-OM-43 hard stop on deleting a lead that owns a quotation, disqualify instead. Build Note: Linkage. The quotation created from a lead carries the lead reference for funnel reporting. The prior build panel reads the same design library index that ENG-REQ-001 uses, so sales and engineering answer the repeat question from one source. Installed base records surface in the prior build search when the searching attribute set matches a shipped unit. OM-CUST-001/2		•		Customer	Master

The downstream quotation, order and delivery carry the money Build wave and field spec Wave 5  •  Fields and rules in This Book §5.1 
5.1	Spares	Finder,	Resolution	Rules • Resolution order. The serial resolves to the installed base record, the installed base record resolves to the as built genealogy, and each genealogy line resolves to the current material master state. The screen therefore shows what is inside that specific unit, not what the current BOM revision says. • Supersession. Where a built part has a successor in the supersession register, both appear, the built part grayed with its identity intact and the successor as the default orderable line. The user may force the original where stock remains and policy allows. • Pricing and entitlement. Prices come from the spares price list through the condition model of Book IV §5. Before quoting, the entitlement service runs. When the unit is under warranty and the requested part relates to an open or attributable failure, the screen must warn and offer the RMA path instead of a sale. • Availability. Stocked SPARE materials show ATP. Non stocked lines show the info record lead time. Lines with neither must route to an engineering review task before the quotation can issue, so no promise is invented. • Compliance. Lines flagged export controlled or hazardous carry their badges and block quotation release until the compliance checklist on the quotation is complete. • Traceability forward. Every spares quotation line stores the serial reference, so the trace explorer later answers which spares were supplied to which unit, in both directions. Build Note: Linkage. This screen composes three existing authorities and adds none. The installed base of Book IV §7.4, the trace record of Book IV §4.4 and the material, pricing and info record masters of Books II and IV. The field service module of Book IX consumes the same resolution rules for van stock and service order parts, so the logic must be built once as a service and called from both places. 6. 6.1	Sales	Kit	BOM Accessory kits sold as one catalog line, commissioning kits, installation hardware sets, recommended spares packages, must be formal BOM objects, not order notes. The rules below are the complete authority for the sales kit and they reuse the existing editors and postings without exception. Aspect Rule Kit header A material of type KIT. Non stocked and non valuated by default, so the header is a commercial wrapper. A stocked variant is permitted for prepacked kits, in which case the header behaves as a normal finished material and the rules below do not apply Kit structure A BOM with usage Sales, single level, components only, no phantoms and no nested kits. Maintained in the EBOM editor with the usage selector set to Sales and released through the same review path as any BOM revision Order entry Entering the kit on a quotation or order explodes it into component sub lines under the kit line. Pricing defaults to the header through the condition model, with a component priced option per price list where the commercial team wants itemized kits. The margin panel evaluates the kit as one unit either way Availability ATP runs on the components, never on the non stocked header. The kit line confirms at the date the last component confirms Delivery and packing Components pick individually. The pack station groups them under one handling unit and the packing list prints the kit header with its components indented. Serialized components scan at SP-16 against the kit line so genealogy holds Postings Cost of goods posts per component at PGI through template #25. The non stocked header never carries value, so no kit level valuation, variance or count exists Returns RMA lines are raised at component level and carry the kit and order reference, so a partial kit return is normal processing Costing view A report only rollup shows the kit standard as the sum of component standards for margin analysis. No cost estimate object is created for a non stocked header Build Note: Linkage. Where used from any component lists its kit memberships. The recommended spares package for a transformer design is itself a sales kit referenced from the engineering request deliverables checklist, which connects this object to the Spares Finder in §5.1 of this Book. 7. This section carries the module in one place, as a designer and builder would consume it. The picture shows how the two halves connect. Costing produces the standard that valuation and variances measure against. Pricing produces the selling price through fixed condition steps. Margin floors sit between them and margin analysis closes the loop after settlement. 
 Figure V-18. The costing and pricing engine room. Left builds cost truth, right builds price truth, the floor gate and margin analysis connect them. 12.1	The	Calculation	Contracts	in	Brief Calculation Rule in One Line Full Spec Work center rates Planned cost center money divided by planned productive hours, released yearly with the budget Book I I §2 Standard cost rollup Material per the MBOM with scrap, labor and overhead per the routing at released rates, rolled bottom up by low level code Book I I §1, §3.2 
ETO estimate Driver quantities such as core and copper weight times maintained driver rates when no design exists yet Book I I §3.1 Price determination Condition steps 10 to 50. Base price by precedence, one discount, surcharge, freight treatment, tax. Elements persist on the document Book I A §4.4, Book IV §5 Commodity surcharge Index at pricing date minus base index, times content per unit, times pass through percent Book I I §6.4 Margin floor Product line floor percent with an escalation chain on any breach Book I I §6.3

Book VII closes the final readiness findings. It proves, bullet by bullet, that the nine items in the Post Inventory Design Review gap register are specified in this document and shows exactly where. It adds the supplemental screen entries those items were missing in the implementation plan. It specifies multi plant planning with the setup screens the single plant model lacked. It records the build package manifest decision and the manifest itself. It describes the delivered migration workbook with its cleansing rules, transformation logic, opening balance rules and reconciliation scripts. It completes the barcode sample book with every label in the system and a usage matrix stating where and when each is printed and scanned. It ends with the go live readiness statement and the execution directive. Nothing earlier in the document is removed or altered. 
1.	Findings	and	Dispositions Finding Raised Disposition MRP, finite planning and CTP specified for a single plant only, with no place to set up more Closed in §4. 4.4	Network	Planning	Behavior	(binding	once	active) • Sourcing per material and plant. Each plant level planning view states how that plant obtains the material. Own production, transfer from a named plant over a lane, or purchase. The sourcing table allows a ranked sequence, and MRP consumes it in order. • Network netting. MRP still runs by low level code, and a transfer source turns demand at the receiving plant into lane offset demand at the supplying plant. Proposals carry both plants and the lane. Exception EX-10 names unworkable network demand, such as an inactive lane or a supplying plant without a valid production version. 

=== PAGE 284 ===
ENTERPRISE ERP — UNIFIED BUILD SPECIFICATION  |  V1.0 CONFIDENTIAL 
CONFIDENTIAL Page 284 of 312 
• Global ATP and CTP. The availability check walks the sourcing sequence. Local stock first, then supplying plant stock plus lane transit, then production CTP at the plant owning the production version. The promise names the sourcing plant and lane so the commitment is auditable, and the plan snapshot rule of Book III Part E applies unchanged. • Cross plant genealogy needs no new design. Events already stamp plant, and STO issue and receipt write movement events on the same chain, so forward and backward traces cross plants natively. The recall population derivation of FL-08 is network correct by construction. • Finite scheduling stays per plant. Each plant schedules its own work centers. The network connects them through dated transfer demand, not through one global schedule, which keeps the engine deterministic and the freeze horizons local. 
5.	Controlled	Build	Package	Manifest The finding offered two remedies. Merge the released Inventory and Accounting volumes into this document, or issue a controlled manifest. The manifest is the correct remedy and is recorded here. The two volumes are released specifications already under change control with a build in progress against them. Absorbing them would reset their approval state, break the section references the build teams use daily, and double the size of this document. The manifest gives the single controlled baseline the finding actually needs. Any change to any manifest row issues a new manifest revision through change control. # Controlled Item Version Owner Repository SHA-256 (first 16) 1 Enterprise ERP Unified Build Specification (this document) V1.0, Jul 2026 Product Owner Controlled documents vault, /erp/specifications Recorded at release signing, a file cannot carry its own final hash 2 Inventory & Warehouse Module Spec V3.0, Mar 2026 Inventory Module Owner Controlled documents vault, /erp/specifications Recorded at manifest signing from the released binary 3 Accounting Module Implementation Spec V2.0, Mar 2026 Finance Module Owner Controlled documents vault, /erp/specifications Recorded at manifest signing from the released binary 4 Data Migration Workbook V1.0, Jul 2026 Data Migration Lead Vault, /erp/migration Recorded at manifest signing 5 Contract repository (OpenAPI, AsyncAPI, schemas per IF catalog) Tagged releases Integration Lead Source control, /erp/contracts Git tags carry commit hashes 6 Figma design library and module files Published versions UX Lead Figma workspace per Book V §10.4 Figma version history 7 Configuration workbook and seed data V per release Functional Leads Source control, /erp/config Git tags

Book VIII is the operating manual for turning this specification into designed screens and a delivered system. It must be the first Book a designer opens. It supplies the eight screen archetypes every screen in Book VI maps to, the one page business flow from lead to delivery, the process diagrams the earlier reviews demanded, the consolidated warehousing and accounting detail, the requirement specifications for the ten target state capabilities, the reconciliation control register and the implementation package. Nothing elsewhere in the document is removed or altered by this Book. 
1.	The	Design	Method Every screen in this system must be designed as one of eight archetypes. An archetype fixes the skeleton. The Book VI entry for the screen fills in the organs. The referenced field section supplies the cells. A designer therefore needs exactly three inputs per screen and no research. The archetype wireframe below, the seven row implementation entry, and the field table the entry cites. A Figma frame must exist per screen per state, named with the screen ID, and every frame must link its specification section. A frame without that link must fail design review at gate DG-2. Step The Rule 1. Pick the archetype The Type cell of the Book VI entry names it. List, Detail, Workbench, Wizard, Kiosk, Dashboard, Config or Dialog 2. Copy the archetype frame The wireframes in §2 are the skeletons. Zones must not be invented per screen. A screen needing a zone the archetype lacks is a change request against this Book, not a local improvisation 3. Fill zones from the entry The Build the layout cell names what goes in each zone. The Primary actions cell names the buttons and their consequences. The Arrives from and Creates cells define the navigation in and out 4. Pull fields from the cited section Field names, types, requiredness, validations and error codes come from the cited section verbatim. Labels must not be paraphrased 5. Design all nine states Normal, empty, loading, validation error, authorization error, conflict, offline, degraded, read only. The states are deliverables, not variants 6. Wire the flow prototype Each of the five BPMN flows in §4 must exist as one Figma prototype wired from the same frames, used directly in UAT scenario reviews 
2.	The	Eight	Screen	Archetypes These wireframes are binding skeletons. The counts beside each name state how many Book VI screens map to it, so the reuse mathematics is visible. Build each archetype once as a Figma component set, then produce screens by instantiation.

Question Binding Answer Bought materials Moving average by default. Receipts re-average, issues post at current average, invoice differences correct stock while coverage exists Made materials Standard cost, released only by costing runs. Receipts at standard, variances split by cause at settlement, revaluation only at release Special ownership Consignment carries no value until withdrawal. Customer property never carries value. Book IX raises the nine target state capabilities from decision register rows to module grade specifications. Each section below must be sufficient to design and build its module without further requirements work. Each module must run on hooks that already exist in Books I through VIII, must add no parallel logic beside an existing engine, and must activate only when its evidence section passes under change control. 1.	Periodic	Actual	Costing	and	the	Material	Ledger The ledger must compute what every material actually cost each period and must revalue inventory and cost of sales to that truth at period end, while standard cost remains the operational yardstick during the period. Nothing may post at actual mid period. 1.1	Objects Object Required Content Ledger period record, one per material, plant and period Beginning quantity and value at standard and at actual. Receipt lines by category, purchase receipts corrected to invoice price, production receipts at settled order actuals, transfer receipts at sending plant actual. Consumption lines by category, production issues, sales issues, scrap, transfers out. Collected single level differences. The periodic unit price. Distribution results, to consumption, to ending inventory, not distributed, not included Difference categories Price differences from PPV and IPV, exchange differences, settlement variances by cause, transfer differences. Each category must remain reportable separately through the rollup Valuation views Two parallel views per material and period, legal at standard with variances expensed as today, and periodic actual. The actual view must never leak into operational documents. Reports must name their view Rollup graph Consumption relationships per period by low level code, built from the genealogy and order records already captured, so multi level distribution follows real consumption, not the static BOM 1.2	Calculation,	Binding	Order • Step one, single level. Periodic unit price equals beginning actual value plus receipt actual value, divided by beginning quantity plus receipt quantity. Differences allocate pro rata between consumption and ending inventory. Zero or negative denominators must park the material in the not distributed category with a worklist entry, never a division fault. • Step two, multi level. By ascending low level code, differences allocated to consumption roll into the receiving materials of that consumption and re enter their own single level pass. Circular consumption within a level must resolve iteratively to a stated tolerance of one cent per material. • Step three, revaluation preview. Inventory delta per material equals ending quantity times the difference between periodic unit price and standard. Cost of sales delta follows the allocated consumption. The preview must show both by valuation class before anything posts. • Step four, posting. One revaluation journal per entity at period close, reversing option for the actual view where the Controller elects reversal accounting. New event keys MLINV and MLCOGS must resolve through the account determination matrix. 1.3	Screens 

=== PAGE 305 ===
ENTERPRISE ERP — UNIFIED BUILD SPECIFICATION  |  V1.0 CONFIDENTIAL

The run must be restartable per step without recomputation of finished steps ML-MAT-001, material price analysis Detail The full period record of §1.1 for one material, every receipt and consumption line with its source document link, the periodic unit price derivation shown as arithmetic a cost accountant can check by hand ML-CONFIG-001 Config View activation per entity, difference category mapping to matrix rows, reversal election, tolerance values, all effective dated with release ceremony Posting at close, actual view revaluation Debit Credit Amount Basis Notes 13xx by valuation class or MLCOGS expense MLINV offset per matrix Preview deltas per §1.2 step three One journal per entity, close checklist step 8b, reversal per election Activation evidence. 2.	The	Advanced	Scheduling	Optimizer A solver must replace the seeded heuristic behind the identical constraint model. The optimizer changes solution quality. It must never change what is feasible, what is frozen, or who decides. 2.1	Requirements • Model class. Constraint programming or mixed integer over operations, work centers, batch cycles, setup families, crews and skills, tools, lanes and maintenance windows, exactly the constraint model already specified. A constraint absent from that model must not be encoded privately in the solver. • Objective. A weighted sum the planner can see and set per horizon, weighted lateness against promises first by default, then setup time, then work in process, then overtime. Weight sets must be named profiles under change control. • Determinism. Fixed seed, fixed time box, best found solution returned with its bound. The same inputs and profile must reproduce the same schedule. • Guardrails. Freeze horizons are absolute. Published promises may not degrade without an at risk record. Every solver move that overrides a planner preference must list in the run log with its objective contribution. • Benchmark. A maintained dataset of real order books must score every solver version against the heuristic on lateness, setup hours and makespan. A version that does not beat the heuristic on the weighted objective must not publish. 2.2	Screens Screen Type Must Contain OPT-RUN-001, optimization runs Wizard Profile selection, horizon, time box, run progress with incumbent objective, compare view against current published schedule with KPI deltas and a per order promise impact list, publish with the standard diff ceremony 

=== PAGE 306 ===
ENTERPRISE ERP — UNIFIED BUILD SPECIFICATION  |  V1.0 CONFIDENTIAL 
CONFIDENTIAL Page 306 of 312 
Screen Type Must Contain OPT-PROF-001, objective profiles Config Named weight sets, effective dating, approval by the planning manager OPT-BENCH-001, benchmark board Dashboard Version scores over the benchmark set, trend per KPI, the gate verdict per version Activation evidence. Benchmark pack results beating the heuristic, plus one month of shadow running beside it with planner decision logs showing no guardrail breach. 
3.	Full	Global	Multi	Plant	ATP	and	CTP The single plant promise machinery and the network objects already exist. This module must make one availability service answer across every plant, lane and entity, and must make every cross network promise as auditable as a local one. 3.1	Requirements • Check sequences must be configuration per material group and customer class. Each sequence is an ordered walk, own plant stock, named partner plant stock plus lane transit, production CTP at each plant holding a valid production version, purchase lead time as the last resort where policy allows. • Every step of the walk must respect the availability definition already binding, and cross entity steps must respect intercompany pairing rules including transfer price validity before a promise may rely on them. • The promise record must name the sourcing plant, the lane, the plan snapshot and the check sequence version. Soft reservations must hold capacity and stock at the sourcing plant, decaying exactly as local reservations decay. • A global backorder run must re promise across the network after every planning cycle, honoring priority classes globally, with reallocation between plants demanding the same approvals as reallocation between orders. • Substitution across plants must follow approved alternates only, and customer visible substitutions keep their confirmation rule. • Performance. A single line global check must return within ten seconds. The nightly global re promise must complete inside the batch window at the two plant volumes. 3.2	Screens Screen Type Must Contain GATP-SEQ-001, check sequences Config Sequence rows per material group and customer class, effective dated, simulation of a sample check against the draft GATP-SIM-001, global availability cockpit Workbench One material across all plants, stock, inbound, capacity slots, lanes, the walk result with each step accepted or rejected and why Promise dialog, extended Dialog The existing dialog must additionally show sourcing plant and lane, and must let sales prefer a plant where commercial terms demand it, logged Activation evidence. The network test packs for planning, intercompany and recall green in the two plant configuration, plus a promise audit of fifty cross plant confirmations tracing to their snapshots. 
4.	Supplier	and	Customer	Portals 

=== PAGE 307 ===
ENTERPRISE ERP — UNIFIED BUILD SPECIFICATION  |  V1.0 CONFIDENTIAL 
CONFIDENTIAL Page 307 of 312 
The portals must be external skins over the same services, the same authorization engine and the same validations as internal screens. A portal action that could bypass one internal rule disqualifies the build. 4.1	Architecture	Rules • Separate external identity tenant, invitation based onboarding, per company accounts with named users, multi factor enforced. Internal and external identities must never share a namespace. • Every portal call must carry the partner scope. Row level security must confine a vendor to its own POs and a customer to its own orders, structurally, not by filtering in the page. • All writes must flow through the public APIs of the contract repository with idempotency keys. Rate limits and full audit as third party disclosures apply. • Availability tier two. Portal outage must never block internal operations, and every portal transaction must have an internal fallback path. 4.2	Supplier	Portal	Screens Screen Must Contain Writes PO worklist and detail Open POs with lines, schedules, STP version chip and drawing package download with checksum shown Acknowledgement per line, quantity, date, price, and explicit STP version acceptance or a deviation request Deviation request Structured alternate or concession proposal against a PO line and STP characteristic Creates the supplier deviation record routed to engineering and quality ASN creation Shipment header, lines against open PO schedules, batch or heat and certificate upload per line, package structure Creates the inbound ASN that pre populates the GRN Invoice and payment status Match state per invoice, discrepancy reasons in plain words, remittance history None. Read only Scorecard On time and quality ratings, open SCARs with due dates SCAR responses in the 8D structure 4.3	Customer	Portal	Screens Screen Must Contain Writes Order status Orders with schedule lines, promise dates, milestone strip for ETO, at risk flags with plain language reasons None 
Drawing approvals Transmittal packages with the released renditions, comment and markup capture Approved, approved as noted, or revise and resubmit, driving the design milestone exactly as the internal capture does Shipment tracking Deliveries, HU list, carrier references, POD state, document dossier download, packing list, CoC, FTR POD acknowledgement where the customer signs electronically Returns RMA request initiation against shipped serials with reason codes Creates the RMA request in Draft for internal approval, never an authorized RMA directly

Element Binding Requirement Signal map Configuration per work center, machine tag to system meaning, cycle count, state run idle down setup, alarm code, measured value to a capture template field. Unmapped tags must log and never post Machine state model One current state per machine with a full state history, timestamps at the edge, reason required for down states per §6.2 Test set interface Result files or direct reads from the test bench must fill the final test capture templates with instrument identity bound, the existing calibration interlock unchanged 6.2	Downtime	and	OEE	Rules • A down state beyond a configured grace must open a downtime record automatically. The operator must code the reason at the terminal within the shift, from the maintained reason tree, planned separated from unplanned. Uncoded downtime must escalate to the supervisor and must not silently age out. • OEE must compute per work center per shift from posted data only. Availability equals run time over planned production time from the capacity calendar. Performance equals ideal cycle count over actual against the routing standard. Quality equals good units over total from confirmations. No side spreadsheets and no manual OEE entry may exist. • Machine counted quantities must reconcile daily against operator confirmations, differences beyond tolerance land on the production reconciliation worklist before settlement. • A machine in a down state must flag its dispatch queue and warn the scheduler, and a hard down beyond a threshold must raise a maintenance order once module eight of this Book is active. 6.3	Screens Screen Type Must Contain MI-MAP-001, signal mapping Config Tag rows per machine with meaning, template field, tolerance, effective dating and a live tag value test panel DT-LOG-001, downtime log Workbench Open uncoded downtime first with aging, reason coding inline, shift and machine filters OEE-DASH-001 Dashboard OEE with its three factors per work center per shift, loss waterfall from the reason tree, drill to downtime and confirmation records in three clicks Activation evidence. One pilot work center reporting a full month from signals, OEE reconciled against operator confirmations, uncoded downtime under five percent of down minutes.

9.	Broader	International	Localization Each new country must be an activation kit assembled from one framework, never a fork. The framework must hold five layers, compliance, tax, documents, language and currency, and a country goes live only when all five pass its checklist. 9.1	The	Five	Layers Layer Binding Requirement Compliance matrix Registrations, statutory books and filing calendar per country, approved before the first transaction, owned by the Controller. The matrix row is the activation trigger Tax regime VAT and GST regimes must run through the existing determination precedence with the tax engine, registrations in the nexus register, reverse charge and import tax rules as regime configuration, e invoicing mandates as adapters on the tax interface, clearance models before invoice issue where the country demands it Documents Invoices, delivery notes, labels and statutory forms must localize through template variants bound to the entity and country, sequential numbering rules per jurisdiction through the number series engine, no hard coded formats Language The text tables already keyed by language must carry translations for customer and vendor facing content, screens remain English at first activation unless the steering decision funds full localization, printed documents must follow the partner language Currency Functional currency per entity as today, daily rates, statutory revaluation runs per country rules, group reporting currency in consolidation 9.2	Screens	and	the	Playbook • LOC-COUNTRY-001, the country activation cockpit, a governed configuration screen holding the five layer checklist per country with owners, evidence links and the go decision record. • Regime configuration extends the existing tax screens, effective dated, simulated before release like every governed configuration. • The first activation must follow the written playbook, entity and books, registrations, regime configuration, template variants, translated texts, opening balances, a statutory close simulation, then the gate. Activation evidence. The first country closes one statutory period with returns generated from the system and reconciled, e invoicing accepted by the authority where mandated, and the checklist archived as the template for the next country. 
10.	Activation	Governance	for	This	Book • Each module above must enter the backlog as its own epic set with the DG gates unchanged, its screens instancing the eight archetypes, its interfaces entering the catalog with contracts, and its workflows entering the register. • No module may activate outside change control against the manifest, and no module may weaken a go live invariant, the posting matrix, the availability definition, the traceability rules and the reconciliation register bind these modules exactly as they bind the core. • Sequencing must follow dependency, not appetite. Machine integration before OEE claims, plant maintenance before predictive ambitions, global availability before global promises are sold, the ledger before any margin statement built on actuals.
